This is the second and last allocated round (completed ceiling 27).\n\nAccepted sources used: b98bb807d514e80d (single-cut and Huber bounds),\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a (ray feasibility and objective), e9a55f57461f253c (the\nstronger fixed H6 reference), and 04219554e41d9750 (the unconditional\noriginal-denominator limit Z_1). No conditional seed from the last fact is\nassumed. C93--C95 are motivation, never proof premises. All new assertions\nused in this draft are proved together below.\n\n## 1. Static model, physical word, and parameters\n\nFix an integer m>=6. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+1/m),\nbeta=1-1/m, a=beta/c, and ell=1+a. Define H_0=() and\n\n    H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a*c^k,H_k), 0<=k<m.\n\nEvery subword uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Let\n\n    j=2^(m-1)-1, n=2^m-1, P=sum H_(m-1),\n    r=a*c^(m-1), b=1+r, d=1/(P+1), sigma=r*d/(2P+1),\n    Kcut=r^2*d^2/(2P+1),\n    p=12/13, q=5/13, rho=q^2/p^2=25/144,\n    kappa=1/ell, J=1+rho*ell, w=1/(2p^2)=169/288.\n\nThe symbol Kcut is the accepted central single-cut value on this exact word;\nit is not the entire worst-case functional W_n and need not equal the Huber\nvalue. Choose dimensionless constants\n\n    0<epsilon<=1/4, |eta|<=1/4.\n\nFor positive Rminus,Rplus define phi(0)=0 and\nphi'(s)=clip(s,[-Rminus,Rplus]). Explicitly phi(s)=s^2/2 in the core,\nphi(s)=Rplus*s-Rplus^2/2 on the positive outer interval, and\nphi(s)=-Rminus*s-Rminus^2/2 on the negative outer interval.\n\nUse u_+=(p,q), u_-=(p,-q), positive radii\n\n    Rplus_+=p*d*(1+epsilon), Rplus_-=p*d*(1-epsilon),\n    Rminus_+=p*sigma*(1+eta), Rminus_-=p*sigma*(1-eta),\n\nand the corresponding two functions phi_+,phi_-. Define ONE static function\n\n    F(x,y)=kappa*y^2/2+w*phi_+(p*x+q*y)+w*phi_-(p*x-q*y).\n\nRun actual GD from X_0=(1,0) using L=1 and exactly the word H_m. There is\nno objective switch, stepsize adaptation, or local curvature rescaling.\n\n## 2. Global legality and the actual minimizer\n\nThe useful uniform parameter bounds are\n\n    2/7<a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, 9/7<ell<3/2,\n    0<kappa<7/9, 0<rho<1/5, 1<J<13/10,\n    P>31, r>9, b>10, b/r<10/9.                     (C96.1)\n\nIndeed a=(m-1)/((m+1)*lambda)>=5/(7lambda)>2/7 because\nlambda<5/2; also a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. There are at least 31 steps in\nH_(m-1), all greater than one. Finally c>2 gives\nr>(2/7)*2^5=64/7>9. The remaining inequalities follow by substitution.\n\nFor any two scalar arguments, the difference of a clip is theta times\nthe difference of those arguments, where 0<=theta<=1. Thus the difference\nof gradients at X,Y equals D(X-Y), where\n\n    D=diag(0,kappa)+w*theta_+*u_+*u_+^T+w*theta_-*u_-*u_-^T,\n    0<=D<=C=diag(1,kappa+rho)<=diag(1,137/144)<=I.\n\nThe Euclidean norm of D is at most one, proving global 1-smoothness.\nEach summand is convex, proving convexity of F. Every summand is\nnonnegative. Since the two nonparallel ridge vectors span R^2 and each\nphi vanishes only at zero, argmin F={0} and min F=0. Consequently the\ntrue initial squared distance is exactly one and the payoff is V=2F(X_n).\n\nThe quadratic background is singular, so it has no positive curvature\nfloor on the full active plane. The total core matrix C is not I. These\nproperties follow from exact algebraic inequalities, not rounded clipping.\n\n## 3. Exact prefix and its central queried gate\n\nWrite X_i=(x_i,y_i). Put\n\n    delta=epsilon*p*d, zeta=eta*p*sigma,\n    y_plus=-epsilon*(q/p)*d*ell,\n    y_minus=eta*(q/p)*sigma*ell,\n    Lcut=epsilon*d*J/2.\n\nAs long as both ridges are in the positive outer intervals, the gradient is\n\n    grad F=(d,kappa*y+delta*q/p^2).\n\nThe FIRST physical step is ell=1/kappa. It sends y_0=0 exactly to y_plus,\nthe stationary value of this affine scalar recursion. Every subsequent\nstep preserves y_plus until a ridge changes gate, irrespective of its size.\nThe proposed prefix is therefore\n\n    x_i=1-d*sum_(t<i) h_t,\n    y_0=0, y_i=y_plus for 1<=i<=j.                 (C96.2)\n\nHere is an induction validating every proposed gate, rather than assuming\nthe affine formula remains applicable. At i=0 both projections are p, while\nRplus_+<=5p/(4(P+1))<p. For 1<=i<j, the remaining prefix mass includes its\nlast step ell, so x_i-d>=ell*d. Hence\n\n    p*x_i+q*y_plus-Rplus_+\n       >=p*d*(ell-epsilon*J)>0.\n\nThe other projection is even larger than p*x_i, and Rplus_-<p*d. This\nproves that both ridges remain strictly positive outer before index j.\nThus (C96.2) follows from the ACTUAL recursion, and x_j=d.\n\nAt index j the first ridge projection is\n\n    p*d+q*y_plus=p*d*(1-epsilon*(J-1)).\n\nIt is positive because epsilon*(J-1)<3/40, and strictly less than\nRplus_+. It therefore lies strictly in that ridge's core. The second\nprojection is p*d*(1+epsilon*(J-1))>Rplus_-, so the second ridge is still\npositive outer. Direct differentiation now gives\n\n    grad F(X_j)=(d-Lcut,-(q/p)*Lcut),\n    x_(j+1)=-r*d+b*Lcut,\n    y_(j+1)=epsilon*(q/p)*d*(b*J/2-ell).          (C96.3)\n\nThe last y-coordinate is positive by b>10 and ell<3/2. In particular the\ncentral gradient is noncollinear with the stationary precentral gradient\n(d,0). The actual gate Hessians diag(0,kappa) and\ndiag(0,kappa)+w*u_+*u_+^T do not commute: their off-diagonal commutator\nhas nonzero magnitude kappa*w*p*q.\n\n## 4. Both ridges stay negative outer throughout the full suffix\n\nFirst verify the point immediately after the central step. By (C96.1),\n\n    x_(j+1)+(q/p)*abs(y_(j+1))\n      <=d*(-r+b*epsilon*J*(1+rho)/2)\n      <d*(-r+39*b/200)\n      <-47*r*d/60.\n\nSince 2P+1>63 and |eta|<=1/4, this is strictly below\n\n    -sigma*(1+|eta|) >= -5*r*d/(4*(2P+1)).\n\nMore explicitly, 47/60>5/(4*(2P+1)), so the last strict comparison has\nthe required orientation. Both ridge projections are therefore below\ntheir own negative thresholds at X_(j+1).\n\nOn the negative outer intersection the full gradient is\n\n    grad F=(-sigma,kappa*y-q*zeta/p^2).\n\nThe FIRST step of the right H_(m-1) block is again ell. It sets y exactly\nto y_minus. Subsequent steps preserve that value as long as the gates\nstay negative outer, while x increases monotonically. The proposed terminal\npoint is\n\n    x_n=-sigma*(P+1)+b*Lcut, y_n=y_minus.         (C96.4)\n\nTo validate all the proposed post-first-step suffix gates it suffices to\ncheck the largest proposed x, namely x_n. The inequality\n\n    x_n+(q/p)*abs(y_minus)<-sigma*(1+|eta|)\n\nis equivalent to\n\n    P-|eta|*J > (b/r)*(2P+1)*epsilon*J/2.        (C96.5)\n\nThe left side exceeds P-13/40. The right side is less than\n13*(2P+1)/72. Their difference exceeds\n(23/36)*P-13/40-13/72>0, because P>31. This proves (C96.5).\nInduction now verifies the complete actual suffix, including its unqueried\nterminal point. It does not replace physical steps by a Hessian-dependent\nschedule. Every one of the slanted ridges remains strictly negative outer\nat every postcentral query and at the terminal point.\n\nThis trajectory NEVER visits the common core intersection. In particular,\nit refutes an unconditional extension of eventual common-core capture to\nall singular-background, nonsaturated-core models. Its poor objective\ncannot be attributed to entering the common quadratic region.\n\n## 5. Exact objective and a whole two-parameter family obstruction\n\nWhen both ridges are negative outer, expanding the defining static function\ngives\n\n    F(x,y)=-sigma*x-(q*zeta/p^2)*y\n           -(sigma^2+zeta^2/p^2)/2+kappa*y^2/2.\n\nNo function-value offsets are deleted. At y=y_minus=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),\nthe transverse linear and quadratic terms combine to\n-q^2*zeta^2/(2p^4*kappa). Substituting (C96.4) yields\n\n    V=2F(X_n)\n     =Kcut-2*sigma*b*Lcut-zeta^2*J/p^2\n     =Kcut*(1-epsilon*b*J/r-eta^2*J/(2P+1)).    (C96.6)\n\nThis is the main joint statement: for EVERY m>=6 and EVERY allowed\nepsilon,eta the single static two-ridge function above is globally legal,\nhas the stated actual minimizer and queried itinerary, and obeys (C96.6).\nThe proof is entirely symbolic and independent of finite trajectory runs.\n\nThe payoff is strictly smaller than Kcut because epsilon>0. V is positive,\nsince the terminal point is strictly in the negative outer region and is\nnot the unique minimizer. As an algebraic check, epsilon*b*J/r<13/36\nand eta^2*J/(2P+1)<13/10080, so the displayed bracket is positive as well.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives Kcut<=K_n(H_m), and accepted\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m). Consequently\nthis entire explicit changing-region family fails to improve even the\naccepted central single cut, for all horizons in its stated domain. A\nnegative tilt eta cannot repair the loss: it incurs the exact additional\nquadratic price in (C96.6). This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\nThe same conclusion prices a free initial transverse displacement as long\nas the initial point (1,y0) lies in both positive outer regions. Its first\nstep sends y0 to the same y_plus, so all later states and terminal values\nare unchanged. Its TRUE initial squared distance is 1+y0^2; its normalized\npayoff is the expression in (C96.6) divided by 1+y0^2. A large transverse\ninitial coordinate cannot be silently removed from the denominator.\n\n## 6. A scalable law on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m sequence\n\nFix epsilon,eta in the displayed ranges independently of m. Let\nM_k=1+sum H_k and r_k=a*c^k. From the physical word,\n\n    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+r_k,\n    M_k=2^k+a*(c^k-2^k)/(c-2).                 (C96.7)\n\nHere c,a still depend on m. As m tends to infinity, c tends to lambda,\na tends to 1/lambda=lambda-2, (2/c)^(m-1) tends to zero, and therefore\nM_(m-1)/c^(m-1) tends to 1. Also (1+1/m)^(m-1) tends to exp(1). Thus\n\n    r/(P+1) -> 1/lambda,\n    lambda^m*Kcut -> 1/(2*exp(1)*lambda),\n    J -> 1+rho*sqrt(2), b/r -> 1.\n\nThe second limit follows directly by writing\nKcut=[r/(P+1)]^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). In (C96.6), the eta term tends to zero.\nWe obtain the fully priced nonzero limit\n\n    lambda^m*V ->\n      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda].   (C96.8)\n\nThe unconditional portion of accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 states that\nZ_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) exists and is finite and positive, for the\nORIGINAL all-split benchmark denominator. Hence\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*V ->\n      Z_1*[1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda]. (C96.9)\n\nOnly that accepted unconditional limit is used; no seed assumption or\nconditional separation from the same fact is imported. Formula (C96.9)\nis the payoff of this exhibited family, not the true worst-case value.\n\nFor comparison with the same-word global Huber witness, S=sum H_m and\nM_m=1+S. Equations (C96.7) give lambda^m/(1+2S)->1/(2*exp(1)), so\n\n    V / [1/(1+2S)] ->\n      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/lambda < 1.\n\nNo finite m=6,7,8 observation is used to prove these limits. Conversely\nnone of those finite cases is described as a test of an m>=128 theorem.\n\n## 7. Bounded discovery, reconstruction, and failure accounting\n\nThe round started from the existing ledger at 16 charged cases, 14 discovery\ncases, one calibration, one exact reconstruction, and 0.6265509749991907\nmeasured/accounted seconds. Calibration against the accepted scalar central\ncut had already passed in case1; it was not rerun or treated as free.\n\nCases17--22 weakened or removed the vertical ridge in the previous rational\npaired-ridge models. All six returned finite trajectories and changed queried\ngates; none exceeded K. Cases23--28 replaced that vertical ridge by a singular\nquadratic background with rational coefficient. All six also returned finite\ntrajectories and no gain. The largest successful new discovery ratio was\ncase23 at approximately 0.8972369014212274*K. Its background has a zero\nhorizontal eigenvalue, so no uniform positive background floor is presumed.\n\nCase29 was a CHARGED FAILED proposal. Its exact algebraic-curvature prefilter\nraised TypeError: \"'>=' not supported between instances of 'Q2' and 'int'\".\nThe raw error, request/source hashes, and public launch event are retained.\nThe run stopped after that error. Defining the missing exact comparison\noperators repaired the implementation; separately charged case30 retried\nthe same proposed static function. No accounting was reset or erased.\n\nCase30 uses epsilon=1/12, eta=0 in the family above. Case31 uses\nepsilon=eta=1/12. Case32 uses epsilon=1/4, eta=0. Their actual two-ridge itineraries are exactly the proposed\ncentral chronology, with changes at indices 31,32 for ridge1 and 32 for\nridge2. Both terminal ridges are negative outer. No finite gain is claimed.\n\nBroad discovery stopped after these cases. Exact Q(sqrt(2)) arithmetic used rational-square sign tests;\ndecimal conversion was only for readable outputs. Both runs checked every\nactual gate, every sampled value/gradient/point, the central and terminal\ncoordinate formulas, and the EXACT identity (C96.6), including its strict\ncomparison with the central cut. Neither is a verifier verdict.\n\nThe cumulative ledger before closure has 34 charged cases: 30 discovery\n(including the one charged error), one calibration, and three exact\nreconstructions. There are 33 finite raw statuses and one error. The two\nremaining discovery slots are retired on allocation closure; reserves are\nfully used. No solver, installation, formal verifier call, C59 replay, or\nbenchmark dynamic program was run. Final metered seconds and hashes are\nrecorded in the companion manifest after closure, not estimated here.\n\n## 8. Same-word references and interpretation\n\nAll forward results retain the global Huber term, every single-cut value,\nthe best single-cut index, and the sufficient first-two-ray comparison on\nthe SAME physical word. The complete finite symbolic argument showing\nF_ray=K for m=6,7,8 is reproduced as an integrated appendix below. It is\njoint draft mathematics, not an invocation of unverified C95 as a fact.\n\nFor m=6 the stronger accepted reference is e9a55f57461f253c:\nW_63(H6)>56/2711>1/50. The exhibited root family is far smaller even by\nan elementary comparison. If t_k=r_k/M_k, then\nt_(k+1)=c*t_k/(2+t_k). Since c>lambda, one has\nc*(c-2)>lambda*(lambda-2)=1>beta and hence a<c-2. Also\nc<=7*lambda/6<35/12<3. Thus a<c-2<1, and induction gives t_k<c-2<1,\nso Kcut<1/(2P+1)<1/63<1/50. The C59 reference is not transplanted to\ndifferent enclosing-m words, and none of its 4160 checks was replayed.\n\nThis work supplies a concrete scoped obstruction and a useful exact relation.\nPersistent outer gates, a singular background, a nonsaturated core matrix,\nand a noncommuting central gate do not by themselves yield improved payoff.\nThe decisive restriction in the explicit solved family is the reciprocal\nshort-step coefficient, which kills the transverse transient at the first\nstep of each child. General coefficients retain a transverse polynomial\nand are outside this exact family theorem. General multilevel gate changes,\nunequal ridge directions/weights, and arbitrary static models remain open.\n\nThe general coefficient relation, useful for a future authorized round, is\nas follows. Retain a fixed kappa>0 and the same symmetric directions/weights\nand radii means. Suppose the ACTUAL chronology is both plus before the\ncentral query, core/plus there, and both minus afterwards. Let\nR=product_(h in H_(m-1))(1-kappa*h), delta=(Rplus_+-Rplus_-)/2,\nzeta=(Rminus_+-Rminus_-)/2, and\n\n    yj=-delta*q*(1-R)/(p^2*kappa),\n    L=(delta-q*yj)/(2p),\n    yc=[1-b*(kappa+rho/2)]*yj-b*delta*q/(2p^2),\n    ye=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),\n    xn=-r*d+b*L+P*sigma, yn=ye+(yc-ye)*R.\n\nSolving the two affine recursions and differentiating the central core gate\ngives these formulas directly. Substitution into the exact static value gives\n\n    V=-2*sigma*xn-2*q*zeta*yn/p^2-sigma^2-zeta^2/p^2+kappa*yn^2.\n\nUnlike (C96.6), this last interface is CONDITIONAL on its stated complete\ngate itinerary. It is not a construction certificate for arbitrary kappa.\nThe exact root family proves that itinerary separately in Sections 3--4.\n\nThe original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile problem remains unresolved.\nNo lower witness on H_m, even if stronger, would alone disprove minimax\noptimality: that negative goal needs an improving schedule's full-class\nupper certificate.\n\n## Appendix: complete same-word finite scalar comparison\n\nThis appendix is included in the present joint derivation. It has no\nunverified predecessor. Here K is the accepted functional K_n(H_m), and\nF_ray is the complete accepted family from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a.\n\nHere is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to\nequal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If\n\n    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)\n\nfor every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.\nIndeed its first two checkpoint gradients are\nm_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.\nThe second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).\nWith exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least\nm_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.\nBoth contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.\nZero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)\nwill be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.\n\n\n\nWrite r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then\n\n    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,\n    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).\n\nFor m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases\nand stays below c-2. Also\n\n    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m\n       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,\n\nso t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.\n\nFor any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio\nR_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix\nbefore i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus\nR_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one\nof u,v is at most m-2. It follows that\n\n    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.\n\nIf u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.\nTheir total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before\nj contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,\nx,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From\n(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain\n\n    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).\n\nLet d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives\n\n    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u\n      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)\n      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).\n\nThe first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient\n((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.\nEquality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge\nand the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening\nprefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus\n\n    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (A1)\n\nThis proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled\npair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three\nauthorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables\nallows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.\n\n* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give\n  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.\n  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.\n* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively\n  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,\n  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.\n* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at\n  its sixth step equal to\n  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because\n  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,\n  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.\n\nAll parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for\nsqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6\nbounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;\nsqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.\nCombining (A1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves\n\n    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (A2)\n\nNo m>=128 result is used here. This finite symbolic comparison is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint\ndraft; it is not an accepted graph fact.\n\nFor completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a\nfixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and\np+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on\nthat interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most\none interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators\nare ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest\noccurrence of level u is best. Its value is\n\n    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].\n\nThe numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.\nThus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly\nthe larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;\nno choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_c96_persistent_outer_gate_exact_loss.md", "raw_summary": "research/c96_round27_forward_summary.json", "accepted_fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "e9a55f57461f253c", "04219554e41d9750"], "plan_id": "2032a0fd3a8973ab", "status": "complete joint unverified draft", "attempt_type": "direct", "subgoal": "price actual weak-background changing-region mechanisms", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "The explicitly proved family never enters its common core, refuting an unconditional capture extension; its exact objective remains scalar-dominated."}, "charged_cases": 34, "discovery_cases": 30, "measured_accounted_seconds": 1.3588828420033678, "raw_failures": [29], "ledger_status": "closed_by_owner", "unused_discovery_retired": 2, "new_fact_submit": 0, "original_target": "OPEN"}, "glossary": {"C96_F": "Static planar function kappa*y^2/2+(169/288)[phi_+(12x/13+5y/13)+phi_-(12x/13-5y/13)] with all radii and parameters defined in the evidence.", "Kcut": "The central single-cut value r^2/[(P+1)^2(2P+1)] on the current physical H_m.", "J": "Local transverse price 1+(25/144)*(1+a), used in the C96 evidence only.", "Lcut": "The central horizontal gradient loss epsilon*d*J/2 in the C96 family."}}

(11)\n\nFor example R^2> (341/100)^2+241/100>14 and\nR^2<(171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200)<71/5.\nSince rho^2>99/100, N>99/242>2/5; also\nN<eta<(201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.\nThe bound e>1/2 follows from (K-1)eta>(141/100)(50/121)>1/2.\n\nConsequently omega>0, Mout>0, and\n\n    Hout>67/5, 1/8<nscale<1/5. Also\n\n    Lcoef/z <= r_0 r_1 N/R^2\n             < (49/20)6(21/50)/14 <1/2.\n\nThus chi(1-Lcoef/z)/nscale >=(5/2)chi, with strict inequality when\nchi>0. Subtracting this from the second bound gives the last part of\n(14). These estimates keep the internal residual cost; it is precisely\nthe term chi(Lcoef-z) that must not be dropped at the critical row.\n\nFor later use, Tfirst,Tsecond<6/5. This follows from Caff<600/599,\nthe second difference bound, and chi<=1/9999. Moreover\n\n    nscale z(Caff-1)<d_atom/2.                      (15)\n\nIf Caff<=1 it is immediate. Otherwise the left side is less than\n(1/5)(201/200)/599=201/599000, while d_atom/2>1/2400;\nthe comparison is 482400<599000.\n\nPut b_j=<p,y_j> and l_j=<w,y_j> for the atom. Their table is\n\n| j | E_j | b_j | l_j |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| 0 | m-1 | R^2-m | D |\n| 1 | z | z Dlen | D |\n| 2 | e | (K-1)z | rho e |\n| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n\nFor j=0,1,2 one has b_j<R^2 and l_j/E_j<=rho. Also\nb_1/E_1=Dlen, while b_j/E_j<(4/5)Dlen for j=0,2.\nFor j=0, the numerator is less than 54/5 and\n(4/5)Dlen(m-1)>(4/5)(29/5)(12/5)>54/5.\nFor j=2, b_2/E_2=K<(4/5)Dlen follows from (11).\n\nFinally the two bounds needed in the reverse orientation are\n\n    sigma r_0 r_1 D m rho/R^4 <1/3,\n    (sigma r_0 D/R^4) Mout <1/10<nscale.             (16)\n\nFor the first, bound its numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)(171/50)\nand its denominator below by 196. For the second, use\nMout<=r_1D and bound the numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)^2.\nBoth resulting rational inequalities are strict.\n\n## 7. Every forward cross row\n\nAbbreviate the following four exact child correlations:\n\n    u_i=<G_i,d>, v_i=<P,G_i>,\n    p_i=<P,Y_i>, q_i=<d,Y_i>.\n\nFrom (6)-(9) their entries are\n\n    u_0=u_1=0,\n    u_2=sigma r_0 rho eta Mout/R^2, u_3=rho u_2,\n    u_4=u_5=k chi m, u_6=-k omega eta, u_7=rho u_6;\n\n    v_0=v_1=m, v_2=z, v_3=D,\n    v_(4+j)=-(sigma r_0 D/R^2)<p,g_j>;\n\n    p_j=b_j,\n    p_(4+j)=-sigma r_0 D(chi+b_j/R^2);\n\n    q_j=sigma r_0 Mout l_j/R^2,\n    q_(4+j)=k[-chi Mout+chi b_j-r_1 l_j],           0<=j<=3.\n                                                               (17)\n\nIn particular p_0,p_1,p_2>0, p_3=0, p_4,p_5,p_6<0,\nand p_7<=0. All q_0,q_1,q_2>0, q_3=0, q_4,q_5,q_6<0,\nand q_7=-k chi Mout<=0. To see the strict late signs, use\nchi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600, while r_1 l_j>1 for j=0,1,2.\n\nEquation (4) and the cross identity (10) give every forward cross row:\n\n    Q_(i,8+j)=aheight_i-c_i-nscale Delta_j+u_i p_j/R^2.\n                                                               (18)\n\nAll aheight_i-c_i are nonpositive. Therefore only positive products\nu_i p_j need further consideration; zero products cause no problem.\n\nFirst let the receiver be j=0,1,2. Positive products can have source\ni=2,3,4,5. For i=4,5, aheight_i=-chi k m and u_i=chi k m,\nso their sum with the mixed term is chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)<=0.\nFor i=2 the ratio of u_2 b_j/R^2 to nscale E_j is\n\n    Tfirst * b_j/(Dlen E_j).\n\nAt j=0,2 it is less than (6/5)(4/5)<1. At j=1, (14)-(15)\ngive Q_(2,9)<=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2.\nFor i=3 the same ratio is multiplied by rho, and (13)-(14)\nmake it strictly smaller than one, including at j=1.\n\nNow let the receiver be 4+j, for atomic j=0,1,2,3. A positive product\ncan only have source i=6 or 7. Formula (18) becomes exactly\n\n    Q_(6,12+j)/k\n       =chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2-nscale E_j-d_atom,\n    Q_(7,12+j)/k\n       =rho[chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2]-nscale E_j.\n                                                               (19)\n\nFor j=3, E_j=b_j=0 and Lcoef<z, so both are nonpositive.\nFor j=0,2 drop the nonpositive chi term and use\nTsecond b_j/(Dlen E_j)<(6/5)(4/5)<1.\nFor j=1 use the last inequality of (14). It gives\n\n    Q_(6,13)/k <=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2,\n    Q_(7,13)/k <=nscale z(rho Caff-1)<0.\n\nReceivers with p_j=0 and products of opposite signs were already\ncovered by (18). This exhausts all 64 forward cross rows, including\nevery row into the actual final sample 15.\n\n## 8. Every reverse cross row and the total original system\n\nThe other orientation is\n\n    Q_(8+i,j)=nscale aheight_i-Delta_j+c_j+v_i q_j/R^2.\n                                                               (20)\n\nFor early receivers j=0,1,2, a positive product requires i=0,1,2,3.\nTheir v_i are at most m. The positive term divided by E_j is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 Mout rho/R^4\n       <=m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nBut Delta_j-c_j>=rho E_j, so (20) is nonpositive. Other source signs\nonly help. Receiver j=3 has q_j=Delta_j=c_j=0, leaving\nnscale aheight_i<=0.\n\nFor a late receiver 4+j, a positive product requires a late source\n4+i. Set Patom_i=<p,g_i>, which is one of m,m,z,D and is positive.\nUsing (7) and (17), combine the source height and mixed term exactly:\n\n    nscale aheight_(4+i)+v_(4+i)q_(4+j)/R^2\n      = k Patom_i {chi[-nscale+(sigma r_0 D/R^4)Mout]\n               +(sigma r_0 D/R^4)[-chi b_j+r_1 l_j]}.\n                                                               (21)\n\nFor j=3 the last bracket is zero; (16) makes (21) nonpositive.\nFor j=0,1,2 discard its nonpositive chi terms. The remaining term,\ndivided by k E_j, is at most\n\n    m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.\n\nThe receiver has Delta_(4+j)-c_(4+j)>=k rho E_j. Thus (20) is\nnonpositive. Early sources have v_i>0 and q_(4+j)<=0, so they are\nalready safe. This checks all 64 reverse cross rows, including rows\nwhose source is the final gradient.\n\nSections 3-4 check all 56 ordered within-left rows; section 3 and\nscaling check all 56 within-right rows. Sections 7-8 check all 128\ncross rows. The 32 optimum incidences hold because every aheight is\nnonpositive and every Delta is nonnegative, including after (1).\nThus all 272 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,15} are proved.\nNo sampled gradient, remote pair, or original position has been removed.\n\n## 9. One global function and the TRUE radius\n\nLet yhat_i=Z_i-J_i and Deltahat_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. On the same R^5\ndefine\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=15)(<yhat_i,v>-Deltahat_i)),\n    F_parent(Z)=sup_v(<Z,v>-H(v)).                  (22)\n\nAll rows just proved, together with accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`,\nshow that this is one finite, everywhere differentiable, convex,\n1-smooth function satisfying F_parent(Z_i)=fhat_i, grad F_parent(Z_i)=J_i,\nand F_parent(0)=0=min F_parent. Its trajectory from Z_0 is exactly (2).\n\nOnly samples 3,7,11,15 have Deltahat_i=0; their residuals are\n0,E_top t,0,B_top t. All other Deltahat_i are strictly positive.\nIt follows directly that\n\n    argmin F_parent=conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}.         (23)\n\nFor completeness, at sufficiently small epsilon>0 in any fixed\ndirection v,\n\n    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2\n       +epsilon max(0,<E_top t,v>,<B_top t,v>).\n\nEvery point of the displayed convex hull has conjugate value zero.\nIf Z is outside it, let z be its closest point and v=Z-z. The elementary\nprojection inequality gives <Z,v> greater than the maximum of the\nthree support values. The displayed local expression then gives\nF_parent(Z)>0. This proves both inclusions in (23).\n\nThe exact nearest-minimizer distance is R. Indeed\n\n    <P,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,\n    <P,d>=sigma r_0 D Mout/R^2>0,\n    <E_top P,B_top t>=<P,d><P,t>/R^2<=0.\n\nHence <Z_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z in (23), and\n||Z_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Z_0,z>>=R^2.\nZero attains equality. At sigma<1 the internal residuals are nonzero;\nthey have not been identified with zero or hidden in a surrogate radius.\n\nThe endpoint value is fhat_15=nscale k N/2. Thus (3)'s exact payoff is\n2F_parent(Z_15)/dist(Z_0,argmin F_parent)^2=nscale k N/R^2.\nRescaling F_parent(RZ)/R^2 makes the true initial distance one without\nchanging this normalized payoff or any step. Multiplying the function\nby a desired L_smooth>0 gives the same normalized payoff with steps\nh_i/L_smooth. This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.\n\n## 10. Paid state, retained value costs, and the next question\n\nThe atom's state is kappa_3=A^2 N/R^2. Its exact defining expressions are\n\n    m=(574+319s)/300, K=(567+622s)/600,\n    z=(145+4s)/150,\n    R^2=(620189+458670s)/90000,\n    N=358801(145+4s)/[90000(567+622s)].\n\nMultiplication using s^2=2 gives\n\n    kappa_3-1=(-8324118905+8781249146s)\n                     /(2305581607500+1614558620000s)>1/2000.\n\nThe denominator is positive. Two thousand times its numerator minus\nits denominator equals 15947939672000s-18953819417500; using s>7/5\ngives the positive lower bound 3373296123300.\n\nThe actual capacities are A_1=lambda A and A_2=lambda^2 A. The seven-\nstep endpoint and the new fifteen-step endpoint satisfy\n\n    N_1=kN, kappa_7=A_1^2 N_1/R^2=sigma^2 kappa_3^2,\n    N_2=nscale N_1, kappa_15=A_2^2 N_2/R^2\n                   =[Hout/(r_1^2 N)] kappa_7^2.     (24)\n\nSince sigma kappa_3>(9999/10000)(2001/2000)>1+1/3000,\nkappa_7>1+1/1500. Put tau=<w_1,t>/N_1=chi D/N and\nnu_res=||t||^2/N_1=chi^2 R^2/N. Bounds (11) give\ntau<3/9999<1/3000, so\n\n    Hout/(r_1^2 N)=1-2tau/r_1+nu_res/r_1^2>1-1/7500.\n\nTherefore kappa_15>(1-1/7500)(1+1/1500)^2>1+1/1000.\nFor the last comparison it suffices to use\n1/750-1/7500-1/(750*7500)>1/1000.\n\nThe actual next boundary includes MORE than these three scalars. Its\ncomplete residual/value data are\n\n    yhat_i=E_top Y_i,\n    Deltahat_i=Delta_i-c_i,\n    aheight_hat_i=aheight_i-c_i,                    0<=i<=7,\n    yhat_(8+j)=B_top Y_j,\n    Deltahat_(8+j)=nscale Delta_j,\n    aheight_hat_(8+j)=nscale aheight_j,              0<=j<=7.\n                                                               (25)\n\nIn particular the two costs in (1) are real intrinsic height deficits\nat queried points, not available a second time for free. At samples 2\nand 6 their incoming predecessor rows from 3 and 7 have been saturated.\nEvery residual and value in (25) must be kept in the next model.\n\nWriting w_2=J_15=B_top w_1 and t_2=yhat_15=B_top t gives\n\n    <w_2,t_2>/N_2=tau, ||t_2||^2/N_2=nu_res.\n\nAt the next physical bridge r_2=lambda A, the outgoing vector is\nd_2=t_2-r_2 w_2, with\n\n    ||d_2||^2=N_2(r_2^2-2r_2 tau+nu_res).            (26)\n\nIf another origin-aligned source-matching join, with endpoint values\nretained, were actually legal, its formal state would be\n\n    kappa_31,formal\n       =(1-2tau/r_2+nu_res/r_2^2) kappa_15^2>1.\n\nThis formula alone is CONDITIONAL. Sections 12-20 below test the\nnonempty extension of the complete state (25), retaining the full\naffine model, all values, a true common minimizer, and a free terminal\ngradient. Their actual construction makes additional value changes;\nthe scalar recurrence by itself certifies none of the original rows.\n\nThe original benchmark ratio in this round is exactly\n\n    V_15/U_15^ZJ=B_15 kappa_15/(lambda^4 A^2).\n\nB_15 is the ORIGINAL all-split denominator of accepted fact\n`b98bb807d514e80d`. No claim identifies it with lambda^4 A^2.\nThe arbitrary-h and non-dyadic gaps remain open.\n\n## 12. The next actual parent and its two value offsets\n\nWe now prove, in this same UNVERIFIED joint candidate, an actual\nthirty-one-step extension of the complete fifteen-step geometry above.\nEvery sigma in [9999/10000,1] is allowed. In particular the genuinely\ntranslated member sigma=9999/10000 is retained throughout.\n\nSet\n\n    r_2=lambda A=(353+248s)/50, c_lift=1/100,\n    n=nscale, P_2=Z_0=E_top P,\n    w_2=J_15=B_top w_1, t_2=Z_15-J_15=B_top t,\n    N_2=||w_2||^2=n k N,\n    d_2=t_2-r_2 w_2.\n\nHere Z_i,J_i,fhat_i are the FULL actual fifteen-step data of section 5,\nincluding both paid deficits at indices 2 and 6. They are not the old\nunmodified-value child. Define\n\n    Mout_j=r_j D-chi R^2,\n    Hout_j=r_j^2 N-2chi r_j D+chi^2 R^2,          j=1,2,\n    q_axis=sigma r_0 D/R^2,\n    Cpair_j=q_axis Mout_j/R^2,                       j=1,2,\n    v_scale=k Hout_2/R^2,\n    t_scale=||d_2||^2/R^2=n v_scale,\n    eps_lift=c_lift chi k D.                         (27)\n\nThus Mout_1=Mout and Hout_1=Hout from section 5. The symbol t_scale is a\npositive scalar; it is distinct from the internal translation vector t.\nUse a blockwise value increment on the fifteen-step child:\n\n    L_i=0,                 i=0,1,2,3,8,9,10,11,\n    L_i=eps_lift,           i=4,5,6,7,\n    L_i=n eps_lift,         i=12,13,14,15.             (28)\n\nSection 14 proves that the modified values fhat_i+L_i remain legal\non the identical fifteen-step vectors. No within-atom interpolation\nrow changes, since all four values in an affected atom increase by\nthe same constant. All the other rows will also be checked.\n\nFor the new top join define actual maps on the five-dimensional child\nspace into the real line direct-sum d_2^perp direct-sum P_2^perp by\n\n    E_2 u=(<u,d_2/||d_2||>, u-<u,d_2/||d_2||>d_2/||d_2||,0),\n    T_2 v=(<v,P_2/R>,0,v-<v,P_2/R>P_2/R),\n    B_2=sqrt(t_scale) T_2.\n\nBoth E_2 and T_2 are isometries. These formulas give\n\n    B_2 P_2=E_2 d_2,\n    <E_2 u,B_2 v>=<u,d_2><P_2,v>/R^2.               (29)\n\nThe ambient space has dimension nine. The thirty-one-step samples are\n\n    Xnew_i=E_2 Z_i, Gnew_i=E_2 J_i, Fnew_i=fhat_i,\n                                                   0<=i<=15,\n    Xnew_(16+j)=B_2 Z_j, Gnew_(16+j)=B_2 J_j,\n    Fnew_(16+j)=t_scale (fhat_j+L_j),                0<=j<=15.\n                                                               (30)\n\nThe physical word is h_31=(h_15,1+r_2,h_15). Its bridge equation is\nXnew_16=E_2(t_2-r_2 w_2); every other update follows from the child.\nAll vectors at all original indices are retained. The final gradient\nis a permitted choice of the full model's free unqueried vector.\nEquation (29) factors the entire Gram, including optimum zero and the\nterminal vector, so PSD and every range condition hold.\n\nThis is an explicit sufficient point of the FULL affine parent model:\nindependent similarities E,F, arbitrary cross Gram, bridge translation\nE d_2-F P_2, every value free, an actual common minimizer, and a free\nterminal gradient. Our choice E=E_2, F=B_2 has zero top translation.\nThere is no assertion that this choice is lossless or forced. The\nnonzero INTERNAL translations remain present in every copied jet.\n\n## 13. Additional rational bounds\n\nWe use the bounds already derived in section 6, and prove here every\nadditional estimate needed for the larger parent. First,\n\n    1+1/2000 < kappa_3 < 1+1/1000,\n    1-rho Caff > 1/10000.                            (31)\n\nThe lower kappa_3 bound was derived in section 10. For the upper bound,\nmultiply its exact positive denominator by kappa_3-1 as displayed there.\nOne thousand times the numerator minus the denominator is\n\n    -10629700512500 + 7166690526000s < 0.\n\nIndeed s<10/7, and\n71666905260000<74407903587500. For the second inequality use\n\n    1-rho Caff=(r_0 r_1 rho-K Dlen)/(r_0 r_1 rho).\n\nThe exact numerator in section 6 is greater than\n84397/45000000, using s>7/5, and the denominator is less than 15.\nThus the ratio is greater than 84397/675000000>1/10000.\n\nThe following bounds will be used without any numerical evaluation:\n\n    14<r_2<15, 0<q_axis<1/5,\n    0<Cpair_1<1/10, 0<Cpair_2<1/4, Cpair_1/n<4/5,\n    99/100<v_scale<101/100,\n    3/25<t_scale<21/100, Cpair_2/v_scale<1/3,\n    c_lift chi D<1/800000,\n    m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4. Define the residual factor\n\n    Fshape_2=Hout_2/(r_2^2 N)\n        =1-2chi D/(r_2N)+chi^2 R^2/(r_2^2 N).\n\nThe coarse bounds in (11) give\n999/1000<Fshape_2<1+1/1000000. Since r_0 r_2=A^2,\n\n    v_scale=sigma^2 kappa_3^2 Fshape_2.\n\nNow sigma^2>999/1000 and (31) give\n(999/1000)^2<v_scale<(1001/1000)^2(1+1/1000000),\nwhich proves the claimed enclosure. The t_scale and Cpair_2/v_scale\nbounds follow using 1/8<n<1/5. The offset bound follows from\n11/9999000<1/800000. Finally\n\n    m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4\n        <(171/50)(49/20)15(11/10)/196<3/4.\n\nFor the positive mixed terms introduce, for j=1,2,\n\n    Lcoef_j=(r_j rho-chi K)eta q_axis,\n    Tfirst_j=Mout_j Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout_j),\n    Tsecond_j=Lcoef_j Dlen/[R^2(k Hout_j/R^2)].         (33)\n\nAt j=1 these are exactly the quantities in section 6. Hence\n\n    Tfirst_1<101/100, Tsecond_1<6/5,\n    Lcoef_1/z<1/2.\n\nAt j=2 we have\n\n    0<Tfirst_2<9/20,\n    0<Tsecond_2<1/2,\n    0<Lcoef_2/z<1+1/1000.                           (34)\n\nTo prove the first estimate, Hout_2>(999/1000)r_2^2N and Mout_2<=r_2D give\n\n    Tfirst_2 < (600/599)(5/12)(1000/999)^2 < 9/20,\n\nusing Caff<600/599, r_2/r_1=lambda>12/5, and sigma>999/1000.\nThe exact difference is\n\n    Tsecond_2-Tfirst_2\n        =chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout_2)<=chi.\n\nIts coefficient is less than one: Hout_2>78, the numerator is less than\n(171/50)^2(59/10)<70, and the denominator is greater than\n(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)78>180. This proves Tsecond_2<1/2.\nLastly,\n\n    Lcoef_2/z=sigma kappa_3-delta r_0D/R^2,          (35)\n\nwhich is positive and smaller than 1001/1000. These scalar estimates\nhave retained chi and the exact outgoing norm Hout_2, rather than\nreplacing the internal residual by zero.\n\n## 14. The lifted fifteen-step values satisfy every internal row\n\nWe must check the RIGHT child in (30), whose values are fhat_i+L_i.\nWithin an atomic block the increments are constant, so every original\nrow there is unchanged. In a seven-step constituent, the late atom\ngains eps_lift while the early atom gains zero, with the corresponding\nscale in the second constituent. The late intrinsic heights are\n\n    -chi k Patom_i+eps_lift <= -(1-c_lift)chi k Patom_i <= 0,\n    Patom_i=<p,g_i> in {m,m,z,D},                       (36)\n\nbefore the already paid nonnegative deficits are subtracted. We used\nD<=Patom_i. The mixed terms at this first internal join are nonpositive,\nas derived in section 3. Thus the early-to-late rows improve; the\nlate-to-early rows stay nonpositive by (36). The deficits at indices\n2 and 6 remain legal: their only adverse receiver changes have the\natomic slacks checked in section 4, and the new constants cancel\nwithin those atoms. Optimum rows also hold: heights are nonpositive\nand energies only increased.\n\nIt remains to check the top cross rows of the fifteen-step child.\nLet lift7_i=eps_lift for i=4,5,6,7 and lift7_i=0 otherwise. Relative to (18)\nand (20), the forward row changes by lift7_i-n lift7_j and the reverse row\nchanges by n lift7_i-lift7_j.\n\nFor a forward row with early source i<4 the change is nonpositive.\nFor late sources i=4,5 and early receivers j=0,1,2, the sum of the\nsource height and positive mixed term is bounded above by\n\n    chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)+eps_lift\n       <=chi k[-m^2/R^2+c_lift D] <= 0.             (37)\n\nHere b_j<=b_0=R^2-m, since b_0-b_1=am>0 and\nb_0-b_2=m(m-1)+z>0. The last bracket is negative by (11):\nm^2/R^2>(341/100)^2/(71/5)>4/5, while c_lift D<11/1000.\nFor j=3 the mixed term is zero and (36) applies. Late sources 6,7\nand early receivers have a nonpositive product. Sources 4,5 and late\nreceivers also have a nonpositive product. Their heights remain\nnonpositive by (36).\n\nThe only other forward cases are sources 6,7 and late receivers 4+j.\nEquation (19) gets the extra term (1-n)eps_lift. After division by k\nthis is at most c_lift chi D<1/800000. At atomic j=0,2, the original\nproof has a negative margin at least nE_j/25>1/400, since its product\nratio is less than (6/5)(4/5)=24/25. At j=1 the source-6 margin is\nd_atom/2>1/2400. The source-7 margin is, by (31),\nnz(1-rho Caff)>1/80000. These are all larger than the possible\nincrement. At j=3 the source-6 expression is at most\n-chi z/2-d_atom and the source-7 expression is at most -chi D/2.\nAdding c_lift chi D leaves both nonpositive, also at chi=0.\nThis covers all forward rows of the lifted child.\n\nFor reverse rows with early receivers, a late source has nonpositive\nmixed term and height bounded in (36); early sources are unchanged.\nThe zero-residual receiver 3 leaves just this nonpositive height.\nFor late receivers, early sources have nonpositive mixed term, and\nthe receiver's increased value helps. If both source and receiver\nare late, the original expression (21) changes by\n(n-1)eps_lift<=0. This covers every reverse row, including all\nendpoint directions. Consequently all 272 original ordered rows of\nthe lifted fifteen-step child, with its optimum zero, are legal.\n\n## 15. Complete boundary table for the actual next join\n\nIndex the four atoms of the fifteen-step child by b=0,1,2,3 and write\nits original index as 4b+j, 0<=j<=3. Put\n\n    s_b=(1,k,n,nk),\n    d_b=(d_atom,k d_atom,0,0).\n\nHere d_b is a value-deficit coefficient, not an outgoing vector.\nFor the atom let Patom_j=<p,g_j>, b_j=<p,y_j>, l_j=<w,y_j> as before,\nand define Zeta_j=<w,g_j>. Their entries include\n\n    Patom_j=(m,m,z,D),\n    Zeta_j=(0,0,rho eta,N),\n    l_j=(D,D,rho e,0).\n\nLet barY_i=Z_i-J_i, barH_i=fhat_i-<J_i,Z_i>+||J_i||^2/2,\nand barDelta_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. The exact intrinsic data are\n\n    barH_(4b+j)=-d_b 1_(j=2),                b=0,2,\n    barH_(4b+j)=-chi s_b Patom_j-d_b 1_(j=2),    b=1,3,\n    barDelta_(4b+j)=s_b E_j-d_b 1_(j=2).            (38)\n\nIn particular barDelta_(4b+j)>=rho s_b E_j for every b,j.\nThe increments (28) are c_lift chi s_b D for b=1,3 and zero\notherwise. Thus every barH_i+L_i is nonpositive, and every\nbarDelta_i+L_i is nonnegative.\n\nWrite\n\n    V_i=<P_2,J_i>, Pval_i=<P_2,barY_i>,\n    U_i=<J_i,d_2>, Qval_i=<d_2,barY_i>.\n\nThe following is a complete four-block table, derived from (6), (10),\nand d_2=B_top alpha T(chi p-r_2 w):\n\n| b | V_(4b+j) | Pval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Patom_j | b_j |\n| 1 | -q_axis Patom_j | -q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n| 2 | Cpair_1 Patom_j | Cpair_1 b_j |\n| 3 | -Cpair_1 q_axis Patom_j | -Cpair_1 q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n\n| b | U_(4b+j) | Qval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 Zeta_j/R^2 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 l_j/R^2 |\n| 1 | Cpair_2 k(chi Patom_j-r_1 Zeta_j) | Cpair_2 k(-chi Mout_1+chi b_j-r_1 l_j) |\n| 2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 Zeta_j/R^2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 l_j/R^2 |\n| 3 | nk(chi Patom_j-r_2 Zeta_j) | nk(-chi Mout_2+chi b_j-r_2 l_j) |\n                                                               (39)\n\nFor example a vector in the left seven-step part pairs with\nB_top alpha T(chi p-r_2w) by multiplying its pairing with the old\noutgoing vector d by <P,alpha T(chi p-r_2w)>/R^2=Cpair_2.\nA vector in the right seven-step part instead gets the factor n.\nThe first table follows by taking the same old cross identity with\nE_top P. These arguments derive every table entry, without a new\nPSD relaxation or a sign assumption on an unlisted coordinate.\n\nThe signs are as follows. In blocks 0,2, Pval is positive for j<3\nand zero for j=3; U is positive only for j=2,3. In blocks 1,3,\nPval is nonpositive; U is nonnegative for j=0,1 and strictly negative\nfor j=2,3. Qval is positive in blocks 0,2 for j<3 and zero for j=3;\nit is negative in blocks 1,3 for j<3, and nonpositive for j=3.\nFor the latter sign, chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600 and r_1 l_j>1 for j<3;\nMout_j>0. At chi=0 the endpoint entries vanish, consistently with\nthe same inequalities.\n\n## 16. Every forward cross row of the thirty-one-step parent\n\nFor any 0<=i,j<=15, (29) and the universal original-row identity (4)\ngive exactly\n\n    Q_(i,16+j)=barH_i-t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j)\n                                      +U_i Pval_j/R^2.          (40)\n\nIf the last product is nonpositive there is nothing to cancel:\nboth preceding terms are nonpositive. We check every possible\npositive product, retaining the zero-energy receivers separately.\n\nFirst suppose the receiver lies in block 0 or 2 and its atomic\nindex is j<3. Its energy is at least rho s_b E_j. The ratios of its\npositive Pval to its block scale are b_j and (Cpair_1/n)b_j, respectively.\nBoth are at most b_j. For sources in blocks 0,2 at atomic indices\n2,3, the mixed term divided by t_scale s_b E_j is at most,\nrespectively,\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tfirst_1 < (1/3)(101/100)<1/2,\n    Tfirst_2 < 1/2.                                 (41)\n\nWe used b_j/E_j<=Dlen, and the endpoint source j=3 multiplies\nthe corresponding atomic-index-2 expression by rho. Since rho>99/100,\nthese products fit within the receiver energy. The other positive\nsources can only be atomic U samples in blocks 1,3. For block 1,\nthe sum of its height and the product is at most\nchi k m[-1+Cpair_2 Pval_j/R^2]<=0. For block 3 it is at most\nchi nk m[-1+Pval_j/R^2]<=0. Here Pval_j<=R^2. Receivers at atomic\nindex 3 in blocks 0,2 have Pval=0 and are already covered.\n\nNow the receiver is in block 1 or 3. A positive product requires\na source at atomic index 2 or 3 in block 1 or 3. For an atomic-index-2\nsource, put s_source=k and theta_source=Cpair_2 if its block is 1, and put\ns_source=nk and theta_source=1 if its block is 3. Let r be r_1 or r_2\naccording to that source block. Let theta_receiver=1 in receiver\nblock 1 and Cpair_1 in receiver block 3. The source height plus product,\nbefore its nonpositive paid deficit is subtracted, is exactly\n\n    s_source { chi(theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r-z)\n             +theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r b_j/R^2 }.   (42)\n\nHere Lcoef_r means Lcoef_1 or Lcoef_2. For an endpoint source this\nwhole expression is multiplied by rho. For atomic receiver j<3,\nthe non-chi part of (42), divided by t_scale s_b E_j, is at most\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tsecond_1 < 2/5       (source block 1),\n    Tsecond_2 < 1/2                   (source block 3).          (43)\n\nFor receiver block 3 these ratios are further multiplied by\nCpair_1/n<4/5. Thus the energy rho s_b E_j pays for the non-chi part.\n\nThe chi part is nonpositive except possibly for source block 3\nand receiver block 1. In that remaining case it is at most\n\n    nk chi z/1000,\n\nby (34). The added receiver energy in (40) is\n\n    t_scale eps_lift = nk chi z (v_scale c_lift rho).\n\nIt is at least the required amount since\nv_scale c_lift rho>(99/100)(1/100)(99/100)>1/1000.\nThe same bound covers the endpoint source, whose coefficient is smaller.\nAt atomic receiver j=3, b_j=E_j=0, so this chi calculation alone\nproves the row. If chi=0 both expressions vanish. This exhausts\nall 256 forward cross rows, including every incoming terminal row.\n\n## 17. Every reverse cross row and the full original system\n\nThe other orientation is exactly\n\n    Q_(16+i,j)=t_scale(barH_i+L_i)-barDelta_j\n                                      +V_i Qval_j/R^2.         (44)\n\nThe height term is nonpositive. Products of opposite signs are\ntherefore already covered. Consider first a receiver in block 0 or 2\nwith atomic index j<3. A positive product requires a source in block\n0 or 2, whose V_i is at most m. Telescoping (1) gives\nR; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).\n\nFor (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients\n(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine\nvalues through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column\nmodulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are\nQ20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are\nQ03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.\nThus (1) gives\n\n D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))\n                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).\n\nBesides (6), direct multiplication of T gives\n\n    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.\n\nAdding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).\n\nThe additional ORIGINAL row used this round is\n\n    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)\n\nIn fact e2=e-J-B and\n2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)\nwithout any PSD quotient or new support assumption.\n\n## 2. Why the old strictly capped equality ray cannot match\n\nFor reference, the earlier three-vector expansion used a positive k\nand theta with\n\n    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta+(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4\n                                                     -k*ell*mu^2,\n    x=b*w-a*theta/2,\n    Z=z-(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta/2.\n\nHere is the expansion, without importing the earlier draft as a premise.\nCombining (4)--(6) and expanding two squares gives\n\n kR-D=||beta*J+x*B||_M^2+k*mu*||Avec-theta*B/(2*k*mu)||_M^2\n       -F(k,theta)*Tcut\n       +[k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*u*theta/2]*B0\n       +beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w)*B1+beta*u*Lambda20\n       +[k*c03old-u*theta/2]*Lambda03\n       +[beta*b*v*c-u*(1+v)*theta/2]*Lambda04\n       +beta*b*v*c*Lambda14+k*c23old*Lambda23.\n\nFor v>0, take the unique root obtained by minimizing F in\n0<=theta<=Ucap:=2*beta*z/(a*u). The root lies strictly between\nk0 and kold, defined below: the unrestricted minimum is already\npositive at k0 and a small positive theta makes F negative at kold.\nThe restricted minimizing theta is min(theta(k),Ucap). Every displayed\ncost is nonnegative. For the B0 cost use its value u*(1-bc) at\nk0,theta=0; the other signs follow from k>k0,\ntheta<=theta(k)<2*k*mu*z, and theta<=Ucap.\n\nOn the strictly capped branch theta=Ucap<theta(k), so F=0 and Z>0.\nIf kR-D=0 and R>0, the positive costs and the two squares force\n\n    ME=-(Z/u)*MB, MJ=-(x/beta)*MB,\n    Lambda20=Lambda03=0.\n\nHere x/beta<1 since bw<=beta and theta>0. Consequently\n\n    M02=(Z/u)*(1-x/beta)*Tcut>0.\n\nThe two zero columns also give Tcut>0: otherwise both J and E would\nhave zero columns and (4), with its positive costs zero, would give\nR=0. But the actual original row gives\n\n    2M02=-Lambda02-w*Lambda04<=0.\n\nThis is a direct sign contradiction. The old relaxation could select\nits Gram energies without satisfying this retained original entry.\nThe following quantitative argument uses (7) to remove its cap.\n\n## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder\n\nAssume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set\n\n    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).\n\nThe minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at\n\n    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).\n\nIt is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,\npartial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum\nis attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is\nb^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,\nwhere gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and\nthe negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.\nThere is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).\n\nHere is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define\n\n    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta\n          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),\n    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),\n    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,\n    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,\n    rstar=u*theta/2.\n\nThe positive Hbar solves\n\n    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,\n\nwhich follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also\n\n    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,\n    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)\n                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)\n\nThe inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied\nby Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.\n\nFull original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal\ndiagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.\nEvery weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence\n\n    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)\n\nWe now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.\nDefine the nonnegative correction\n\n    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)\n                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),\n    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),\n    X=beta*J+x*B.\n\nThe EXACT original-array remainder is\n\n    eps:=k*R-D\n       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2\n         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m\n         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02\n         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)\n\nwhere\n\n    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,\n    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,\n    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,\n    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,\n    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.\n\nTo check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is\n\n beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut\n                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.\n\nSubtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.\nRestore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in\n(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs\nfrom 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the\nuncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).\n\nEvery displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are\nSTRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.\n\nLet\n\n    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).\n\nThen\n\n    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)\n\nIf qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put\ntau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and\nSden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives\n\n    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)\n                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),\n    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).\n\nThe numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality\nqcorr<qmax is equivalent to\n\n    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.\n\nIts left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover\n2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the\nstrict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from\ntau/(1+tau)<1/2.\n\nAlso rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than\n(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than\n(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).\nAt k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;\nit strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.\nEquation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally\nrstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.\nThe definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.\n\nSet\n\n    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,\n    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.\n\nThe positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is\n\n    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)\n\nIn particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. The old\ncap theta<=2*beta*z/(a*u) is unnecessary once (7) is retained. On\nthe old strictly capped branch its root kstar is strictly larger\nthan this k, so an additional algebraic strip kR<D<=kstar*R is\nexcluded. This comparison is only with that relaxation, NOT with the\ncomplete older scalar/triangle/curvature conjunction.\n\n## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget\n\nDefine\n\n    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,\n    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,\n    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).\n\nTp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives\n\n    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,\n    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)\n             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)\n\nFor the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and\nI0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow\ngives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and\nMp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains\n\n pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),\n 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,\n\nwhich gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,\nand 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.\n\nBy (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define\n\n    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),\n\nomitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with\nxi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,\n\n    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)\n\nSubtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients\nare nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the\norder of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.\nThe remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is\n\n    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.\n\nThus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent\nenergy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity\nshows Qplus>=0, and\n\n    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).\n\nThat prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not\ncounted as an independent improvement.\n\nFor completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is\nxi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)\nhas supremum\n\n    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.\n\nOtherwise its supremum is\n\n    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).\n\nThe latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,\nthe supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is\nu*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.\nThese statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is\n\n sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).\n\nThe earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain\n0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where\n\n    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.\n\nIts exact identity is\n\n Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11\n                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set\ngfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on\n\n Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),\n Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).\n\nThe nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,\nQ12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite\nresidual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and\n<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.\nThe energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo\ne are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).\n\nIn particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side\neps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced\nby\n\n min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),\n                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)\n\nThis follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so\nthe same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.\nIt does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,\nputting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives\n\n u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).\n\nFor delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply\nErow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and\n\n    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).\n\nTo see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is\n\n u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)\n    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).\n\nThe second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting\neta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed\nbound. All its hypotheses remain.\n\n## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality\n\nIn this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.\nLet\n\n    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,\n    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,\n    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,\n    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),\n    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),\n    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),\n    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),\n    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).\n\nAll denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original\nterminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.\n\nEvery full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.\nWrite y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set\n\n tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y\n       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))\n                                                            /(2*jcoef),\n Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2\n            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.\n\nThen\n\n                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)\n\nHere is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.\nThe vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is\n\n    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.\n\nThe original terminal rows give\n\n    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,\n    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.\n\nSince X=Y+alpha E, they imply\n\n x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M\n                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.\n\nTaking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives\n\n <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut\n       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)\n       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)\n\nLet eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum\nin (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),\n ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).\n\nAlso ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and\nc14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in\n(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use\n<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is\n\n jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)\n        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)\n\nFor the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give\n\n R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)\n                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C\n  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)\n\nThe cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of\nthe SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.\nThus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.\nReplacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for\ntcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,\n\n jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.\n\nIts nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases\nwith tcut>=0, which proves (17).\n\nThis supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is\ncontinuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,\nand\n\n    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.\n\nConsequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with\nPsi(y0)=R, and every full array must have\n\n                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)\n\nStrict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive\ncoefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition\nand proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0\nforces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound\n(14) alone is zero. This prices a further original cross column.\nNo uniform lower bound as v,ell,R,Q or other factors vanish is claimed.\n\n## 6. COMPLETE original-array matching on the new equality locus\n\nAssume eps=0 and R>0, still with a,b,c>0,bc<=1,0<v<2. Equation(12)\nforces ME=MY=0, hence MX=0, and\n\n    B0=B1=Lambda20=Lambda03=Lambda14=Lambda23=0.\n\nEquation(6) now gives Lambda04=0, and (7) gives Lambda02=0.\nThe first moment in (3) forces Lambda01=2; node0 flow then gives\nLambda10=2A. Its diagonal is m=1-a^2-2a, so\n\n                         a=sqrt(2)-1, a*A=1.                    (22)\n\nSet\n\n    Tstar=R/rcoef>0, r13=2*rho*Tstar, Bphys=Hpre+w, s=Bphys+v.\n\nOn this locus EVERY original multiplier is forced as follows:\n\n    Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2A, Lambda13=r13,\n    Lambda12=(2*A*Hpre-(beta+w)*r13)/beta,\n    Lambda21=2*Hpre+Lambda12+r13,\n    Lambda24=(2*w*N+beta*Lambda21)/(w+v),\n    Lambda42=Lambda24/c,\n    Lambda32=2*Bphys+r13+(c-1)*Lambda42,\n    Lambda43=(2*s*C-(w+v)*Lambda42)/v,\n    Lambda34=(1-c)*Lambda42+Lambda43-2*s,                         (23)\n\nand the other TEN off-diagonal coefficients are zero:\n\n    02,03,04,14,20,23,30,31,40,41.\n\nLet\n\n    nstar=Lambda42+Lambda43-s*(s+2),\n    vcol=(0,-rho,rho-1,1,0)^T, dcol=(0,0,0,-1,1)^T.\n\nFor this array the ENTIRE original matrix is\n\n                  M=Tstar*vcol*vcol^T+nstar*dcol*dcol^T.          (24)\n\nThus a word on eps=0,R>0 has a full original certificate if and\nonly if (22) holds and the explicit array (23) has\n\n    Lambda12,Lambda32,Lambda34,Lambda43>=0, nstar>=0.              (25)\n\nAll other surviving signs then follow from their displayed formulas.\nIn particular Lambda21>0, Lambda24>0, Lambda42>0. This is an\nactual all-twenty-entry matching, not a supplied covariance-fiber test.\n\nHere is the proof of necessity and sufficiency of these formulas.\nFrom E=0 and X=0 in the M seminorm, MJ=-rho*MB. Equations(4),(8)\ngive Tcut=Tstar. The original row <e1,B> gives Lambda13=r13.\nNode1's moment and flow give respectively Lambda12 and Lambda21\nin (23). Since M14=0 and MJ=-rho MB with rho>0, MB has fourth\ncomponent zero. Hence M24=0 too, because e2=e-J-B. The original\nentry 2M24=c*Lambda42-Lambda24 gives their displayed relation.\nNode2's moment and flow give Lambda24 and Lambda32. Node4's\nmoment and flow give Lambda43 and Lambda34. There is no free\ncovariance or multiplier left.\n\nConversely impose (22), eps=0,R>0 and define (23), allowing real\ncoefficients temporarily. The node0,1,2,4 flows and moments hold by\nconstruction. For node3 the flow follows by summing the other flows.\nIts moment can be checked explicitly: substituting (23) gives\n\n -w*Lambda32+v*Lambda34\n   =-2*w*Bphys-w*r13-c*(w+v)*Lambda42+2*s*(C-v)\n   =-2*(w+N)*Bphys+2*s*(C-v)=2*v*(1+Bphys),\n\nwhere c(w+v)Lambda42=2*N*Bphys-w*r13 and\nw+N=C-v=1+Bphys. Thus the full flow and Me=0 hold.\n\nThe original first row vanishes because aA=1; also M14=M24=0,\nM13=-rho*Tstar. Direct substitution of Lambda12 gives\n\n    M11=-D/beta^2+2*b*w*rho*Tstar/beta=rho^2*Tstar.\n\nFor the constant term in this identity use A^2=2A+1:\n1-b^2+2A-2*A*b*Hpre/beta=-D/beta^2.\nFor the second equality use eps=0 and (8), which give\nD=(2*b*w*x-x^2)*Tstar. The kernel then determines M12.\nFinally (4), with all its costs zero and E row zero, determines\nTcut=Tstar: its positive Tcut coefficient is\nell*mu^2+a^2*b^2*mu^3, and (8) gives exactly that same coefficient\nafter subtracting the known J terms from R=rcoef*Tstar.\nMe=0 now fixes every remaining entry except M44=nstar and proves\n(24). The vectors vcol and dcol are independent, so full PSD is\nequivalent to nstar>=0. This proves sufficiency as well as necessity.\n\nThe matched quadratic calibration has no extra freely selectable term:\n\n    Q=jcoef^2*Tstar+dphys^2*nstar.                              (26)\n\nIndeed pquad^T vcol=-jcoef and pquad^T dcol=dphys, by (8). Equation(13)\napplies to the flow-correct array, so (24) proves (26). Consequently\nnstar>=0 is equivalently Q>=jcoef^2*R/rcoef. At Q=0 this is\nimpossible when R>0; the WHOLE zero quadratic column is retained.\n\nNo word at C>=193/25 is exhibited satisfying eps=0 and (22)--(25).\nNor is it proved here that their entire high-capacity region is empty.\n\n## 7. Zero boundaries and the unresolved complete comparison\n\nAt v=0 with ell>0, z=theta=qcorr=0, k=k0=kold=b^2/ell. Identity\n(10) still holds and its Gram has sigma>0, but some displayed costs\nvanish. At R=0 or Q=0, use the undivided norm identities;\nzero M-energy always kills its WHOLE original column.\n\nThe complete older step system includes the compact-domain cuts,\nfull quadratic and return tests, all clipping branches, all adjacent\nand lifted triangles, C82/C84, the entire C83/C86 curvature families,\nthe later lifted return, the2948 first-row test and its stated\nhypotheses, and the previously recorded scoped exclusion regions.\nShared19f1d27c0eb8a588 and554ec17f7f590bf0 were reread to retain this\ncomplete list.
