{"id": "1aff1d7a0b6cc5ec", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T13:29:36.460406+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C97 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED draft: an explicit two-state nonstationary Markov law gives positive affine values, dense signed correlations, real nonzero paid transverse action, ALL original pairs, one static smooth function and exact radius for every word with min h>1. A whole larger class is excluded on original H_m: aligned initial anchor, arbitrary varying 1<eta_i<h_i, tight queried optimum upper rows and arbitrary Markov product correlations imply V<=(a+2)beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m), so original B_n V->0, even with FREE terminal. Exact legal (3,10) segment and a positive-height local-only counterexample are included; arbitrary-h Goal stays OPEN.", "evidence": "# C97: a chronological Markov construction with positive affine values\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Actual runtime round 1 of the\ncurrent two-round wave; baseline 27, completed ceiling 29, attempted ceiling\n30 including the historical interruption. No numerical evaluation, solver,\nmachine algebra, replay, subordinate agent, or formal verifier is used.\nThe original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.\n\nThe only accepted mathematical predecessors used are\n`1252b8823bb5a290`, for the original all-directed-pair interpolation\nconvention and its explicit static conjugate, and `b98bb807d514e80d`,\nfor the original benchmark convention and its crude exact bound.\nAll new results below are proved jointly. No C90--C96 assertion is a\nmathematical predecessor. Probability is a coordinate construction,\nnot a replacement for any interpolation inequality.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the three scoped conclusions\n\nWork with smoothness 1 on a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space.\nFor a supplied finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), actual GD means\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,  g_i=grad F(X_i),\n    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,  V=2f_n/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2.\n\nThe optimum is denoted star, with X_star=g_star=f_star=0. Define the\noriginal residual, for every distinct ordered pair including star, by\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (1)\n\nDefine the positive affine value reserve\n\n    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nHere are the conclusions, each limited to its stated class.\n\nA. Every nonempty supplied word with min_i h_i>1 has the explicit\ntwo-state, time-dependent Markov construction in Sections 2--5. All\nsampled value reserves are positive, the original gradients have dense\nsigned product correlations and real independent innovations, and every\nordered row (1) is proved. One fixed convex 1-smooth function realizes\nthe entire run and has unique minimizer 0 and exact initial distance eta.\nThe normalized payoff and a paid append rule are explicit.\n\nB. For the original recursive diagnostic words H_m, the ENTIRE broader\nclass of queried Markov product correlations with an aligned initial\npoint and tight queried optimum upper rows has negligible original-B\npayoff, under the varying support-ratio conditions of Section 7. This\nallows arbitrary varying positive support ratios, arbitrary correlations,\narbitrary amplitudes, and a completely free terminal gradient and value.\nIt is not only a bound on the displayed safe rule.\n\nC. The exact small example in Section 9 refutes replacing the distant\nrows by adjacent rows, optimum rows, positive affine values, real PSD,\nand even all reverse chronological rows. A remote forward row fails.\n\nNeither A nor B nor C is a theorem about unrestricted W_n(h). In\nparticular min h>1 is already a restriction for A; the additional\ngeometric hypotheses for B are essential and are identified explicitly.\n\n## 2. The bounded rule, genuine joint law, and real coordinates\n\nFix n>=1 and h_i>1. Choose two scalar parameters\n\n    1<eta<min_i h_i,  0<theta<1,\n\nand set a bookkeeping value h_n=eta. This h_n is NOT a physical step.\nFor example, the completely specified choice\n\n    eta=(1+min_i h_i)/2, theta=(n+1)/(n+2)\n\ndepends only on the supplied word. Keeping the two scalar parameters\nvisible makes the continuation law and its bounds clearer. Define\n\n    b_i=(h_i-eta)/eta,\n    t_i=theta*min{1,(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1)},\n    r_i=-sqrt(t_i/b_i),                         0<=i<n,\n    A_0=1, A_(i+1)=sqrt(t_i*b_i)*A_i.                (2)\n\nNo Gram entry is a free parameter. The fixed rule reads only successive\nphysical steps and the two common scalars. Because h_(i+1)>=eta>1,\n\n    0<t_i<=theta<1,\n    t_i/b_i<=theta*eta/h_(i+1)<=theta<1,\n    h_i-t_i*h_(i+1)>=eta,\n    h_i-t_i>=eta.                                   (3)\n\nThe third inequality follows from t_i<=(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1).\nThe fourth follows because h_(i+1)>1. Thus -1<r_i<0.\n\nLet S_0 be a fair sign and let B_0,...,B_(n-1) be independent signs,\nindependent of S_0, with P(B_i=1)=(1+r_i)/2. Put S_(i+1)=S_i B_i.\nThe complete finite joint law on sign strings s=(s_0,...,s_n) is\n\n    p(s)=2^(-n-1) product_(i<n)(1+r_i*s_i*s_(i+1)).   (4)\n\nEvery atom is positive, and summing successively over the last sign\nproves that the probabilities sum to 1. Independent bond signs give\n\n    E[S_i S_j]=product_(k=i)^(j-1)r_k, i<j.          (5)\n\nFor example, literal real coordinates u_i(s)=sqrt(p(s))*s_i realize\n(5). A smaller explicit realization uses the orthonormal basis\ne_0,...,e_n of R^(n+1):\n\n    u_0=e_0,\n    u_(i+1)=r_i*u_i+sqrt(1-r_i^2)*e_(i+1).           (6)\n\nInduction gives ||u_i||=1 and (5) as its actual real inner products.\nThus all PSD requirements are proved by coordinates. Every new\ndirection has a nonzero innovation, and all distinct inner products\nare nonzero with sign (-1)^(j-i). The transition law changes when its\nsuccessive-step formula changes; it is not a fixed one-initial-sign\nconstant-correlation law. Section 6 gives a concrete changing example.\n\nSet, in these SAME coordinates,\n\n    g_i=A_i*u_i,\n    X_0=eta*u_0,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t,\n    q_i=A_i^2,\n    f_i=(eta-1/2)*q_i, delta_i=(eta-1)*q_i.           (7)\n\nEvery q_i and delta_i is strictly positive. These are data at all the\noriginal samples, including the terminal. No subword is reset.\n\nFor later use define, for i<=j,\n\n    P_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k),\n    E_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(t_k*b_k)=q_j/q_i,        (8)\n\nwith empty products 1. Since A_(k+1)*r_k/A_k=-t_k,\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=q_i*P_ij, i<j.                        (9)\n\n## 3. Actual support and every original directed inequality\n\nFirst prove the actual support identity\n\n    <X_i,g_i>=eta*q_i, 0<=i<=n.                     (10)\n\nIt holds at i=0. The new basis vector e_(i+1) is orthogonal to X_i\nand g_i, since both are in span(e_0,...,e_i). Hence\n\n    <X_(i+1),u_(i+1)>=r_i*(eta-h_i)*A_i\n                           =eta*A_(i+1),\n\nwhere (2) was used. This proves (10) by induction. It proves support\nalong the actual GD displacement; it is not an independently assigned\ninner product.\n\nThe optimum rows are now exactly\n\n    Q_(i,star)=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-(eta-1)*q_i<0.                       (11)\n\nFor every i<j, all future innovations in u_j are orthogonal to X_i,\nso <X_i,g_j>=eta*<g_i,g_j>. Expanding (1), (7), and (10) gives\n\n    Q_(j,i)=(eta-1)*q_i*(P_ij-1).                   (12)\n\nIf j-i is odd then P_ij<0. If j-i is even, 0<P_ij<1 by (3).\nThus every reverse chronological row is strictly negative.\n\nFor the other orientation keep the whole physical displacement:\n\n    Q_(i,j)/q_i\n      =eta-sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik-P_ij\n                                      -(eta-1)*E_ij. (13)\n\nNo old step is omitted from this expression. We show\n\n    sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik+P_ij>=eta.              (14)\n\nFor even d=j-i, pair the physical terms consecutively:\n\n    sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik+P_ij\n      =sum_(r=0)^(d/2-1)P_(i,i+2r)\n          [h_(i+2r)-t_(i+2r)*h_(i+2r+1)]+P_ij.\n\nEach even-prefix product is positive. Every bracket is at least eta\nby (3), and the first product is 1. The last term P_ij is positive,\nwhich proves (14).\n\nFor odd d=2v+1, the analogous identity is\n\n    sum_(r=0)^(v-1)P_(i,i+2r)\n          [h_(i+2r)-t_(i+2r)*h_(i+2r+1)]\n        +P_(i,j-1)*[h_(j-1)-t_(j-1)].\n\nIf v=0, the only bracket is at least eta by (3). If v>=1,\nthe first paired bracket is at least eta and every remaining term\nis positive. This proves (14) in every case. Equation (13) is\ntherefore at most -(eta-1)*E_ij<0.\n\nEquations (11)--(14) prove ALL ordered original rows, including every\nold/new cross row and both directions at the unqueried terminal.\n\n## 4. One static function and the true radius\n\nDefine y_i=X_i-g_i and form, once for the complete supplied word,\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-delta_i)},\n    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                          (15)\n\nThis is the explicit full-data conjugate of accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`. To match its notation, expand\n\n    <X_i,g_i>-f_i+<X_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2\n       =||v||^2/2+<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i.\n\nThe established all-pair inequalities therefore imply that F is\neverywhere finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, and that\nF(X_i)=f_i and grad F(X_i)=g_i for EVERY i. Consequently its actual\nGD visits precisely the prescribed samples. The function is fixed\nbefore the first update.\n\nEvery delta_i is strictly positive. The maximum in (15) therefore\nequals 0 in a neighborhood of v=0. Since H(v)>=||v||^2/2, F(0)=0.\nFor any X!=0 choose a sufficiently small s in (0,1) with sX in that\nneighborhood. Then\n\n    F(X)>=(s-s^2/2)||X||^2>0.\n\nThus argmin F={0}, and the true initial squared distance is exactly\n||X_0||^2=eta^2. It has not been replaced by a projected or reset radius.\nThe exact payoff is\n\n    V=(2eta-1)/eta^2 * product_(i<n)(t_i*b_i).        (16)\n\nEvery queried i>=1 produces actual paid transverse action. The point\nX_i has zero e_i coordinate, whereas the new coordinate of g_i is\nA_i*sqrt(1-r_(i-1)^2)>0. Thus the e_i component of X_(i+1)-X_i is\n\n    -h_i*A_i*sqrt(1-r_(i-1)^2),                     (17)\n\nwhich is nonzero. In particular a two-step segment already has a\nnonzero displacement transverse to its first gradient. All of it is\ninside (7), (10), (13), and the distance calculation above.\n\nFor arbitrary smoothness L>0 use L*F and physical steps h_i/L; the\nsame normalized W=2R payoff (16) follows.\n\n## 5. The paid continuation rule and its limit\n\nFor a jointly planned chronological segment [r,s), the amplitude and\nvalue transfer is exactly\n\n    q_s=q_r*E_rs, f_s=f_r*E_rs,\n    X_s=X_r-sum_(i=r)^(s-1)h_i*A_i*u_i.              (18)\n\nThe direction is propagated by (6), keeping the old coordinates.\nThe state consists of the actual X_r,u_r,A_r, the common eta, and the\nlast physical-step guard; no independent Gram block or free mode is\nchosen. The vector dimension grows by the prescribed orthogonal\ninnovation. Its coefficient is fixed by (2).\n\nFor a literal append to already fixed jets 0,...,k, the sufficient\njoin condition inherited from (3) is\n\n    t_(k-1)*h_k<=h_(k-1)-eta,                       (19)\n\nwhen k>=1. Here h_k is the first NEW physical step. Retain all old\njets and transition coefficients, require h_k>eta, and choose every\nnew transition by (2) using the supplied future word. All old guards\nthen remain true and (19) supplies the missing boundary guard.\nThe proofs of (10)--(14) apply to every pair straddling the boundary.\n\nThis proves existence of a single interpolant for the extended full\nhistory. It does NOT mean changing the running objective at a join,\nnor that the old interpolant (15) on a shorter data set already has\nthe required behavior outside its samples. In the intended use the\nentire word, all guards and (15) are fixed before GD starts.\n\nThe old artificial value h_k=eta used to terminate a shorter word\ncannot simply be replaced for free by a larger physical h_k. If (19)\nfails, this sufficient append rule gives no extension claim. The\nexact all-pair formula (13) remains available, but adjacent constraints\nalone cannot repair it, as Section 9 demonstrates.\n\nFor perspective, even the safe family itself is extremely weak on\nthe original H_m of Section 8. Put ell=1+a. Every even physical index\nhas h_i=ell; there are N/2 such steps and the last step is even.\nLet b_low=(ell-eta)/eta<a. Pair each low step except the last with\nthe following step H>ell. Equations (2) give\n\n    (t_low*b_low)*(t_high*b_high)\n      <=theta^2*b_low^2*(H-eta)/H<=b_low^2.\n\nThe unpaired last step has t_last=theta*b_low and contributes\ntheta*b_low^2<=b_low^2, since b_low<a<1. The prefactor in (16) is\nat most 1 because eta^2-(2eta-1)=(eta-1)^2. Thus\n\n    V<=b_low^N<a^N.                                 (20)\n\nThis is only the displayed guard rule. The next result treats a much\nlarger family, without imposing that guard or a constant eta.\n\n## 6. Concrete legal changing segment\n\nFor h=(3,10), choose eta=2 and theta=1/2. Then\n\n    b=(1/2,4), t=(1/20,1/2),\n    r=(-1/sqrt(10),-1/(2sqrt(2))),\n    q=(1,1/40,1/20), f=(3/2,3/80,3/40).\n\nOne exact real realization is\n\n    g_0=e_0,\n    g_1=(-e_0+3e_1)/20,\n    g_2=(e_0-3e_1+sqrt(70)e_2)/40,\n    X_0=2e_0, X_1=-e_0, X_2=-(e_0+3e_1)/2.\n\nThese are actual positions from the stated steps. The reserves are\ndelta=(1,1/40,1/20). All six non-optimum ordered residuals are\n\n    Q_01=-39/40, Q_10=-21/20,\n    Q_02=-23/40, Q_20=-39/40,\n    Q_12=-19/80, Q_21=-3/80.\n\nThe optimum rows are (11). Thus the fixed function (15) realizes\nthe segment, has unique minimizer 0, true squared distance 4, and\nnormalized payoff V=3/80. Its second displacement has e_1 component\n-3/2, and its two transition correlations are different. This is a\ncomplete legal coupled segment, not a correlation-only example.\nIt is not claimed stronger than the older tests on this word.\n\n## 7. A whole-class obstruction with varying positive affine values\n\nThe following hypothesis class is substantially broader than (2).\nLet an ACTUAL convex 1-smooth function with minimum 0 at 0 generate\na nonzero queried trajectory g_i=A_i*u_i, A_i>0, ||u_i||=1,\nfor 0<=i<n. Suppose:\n\n  (i) The initial point is X_0=eta_0*g_0.\n (ii) For every queried i there is 1<eta_i<h_i such that\n\n          <X_i,g_i>=eta_i*q_i,\n          f_i=(eta_i-1/2)*q_i, q_i=A_i^2.           (21)\n\n(iii) There are real r_0,...,r_(n-2) in [-1,1] with\n\n          <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)r_k, i<j<n. (22)\n\nThere is NO constraint on the terminal gradient or value beyond\nbeing generated by the same legal function. No sufficient guards,\nstationarity, common amplitude ratio, common eta, or full-support\nprobability assumption is imposed on this class.\n\nFirst, the true initial distance is exactly eta_0*A_0. Indeed (21)\nat i=0 implies f_0+q_0/2=eta_0*q_0. For any minimizer z, the\nnecessary smooth interpolation row gives\n\n    f_0+q_0/2<=<g_0,X_0-z>=eta_0*q_0-<g_0,z>.\n\nHence <g_0,z><=0 and\n||X_0-z||^2=eta_0^2*q_0-2eta_0<g_0,z>+||z||^2\n>=eta_0^2*q_0. The minimizer 0 attains this distance. Thus the\ndenominator is proved even when the minimizer is not unique.\n\nThe Markov product identity and X_i in span(g_0,...,g_i) give\n\n    <X_(i+1),u_(i+1)>=r_i*(eta_i-h_i)*A_i.\n\nCombining with (21) at i+1 forces r_i<0 and gives\n\n    b_i=(h_i-eta_i)/eta_(i+1)>0,\n    A_(i+1)=-r_i*b_i*A_i,\n    t_i=r_i^2*b_i>0,\n    q_(i+1)=t_i*b_i*q_i,       0<=i<n-1.            (23)\n\nThe b_i in this section are the varying-ratio generalization of (2),\nnot an assumption that eta_i is constant. For i<j<n, (22) also gives\n<X_i,g_j>=eta_i<g_i,g_j>. Full expansion of the ORIGINAL row yields\n\n    Q_(j,i)=(eta_i-1)*q_i\n             [product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k)-1].         (24)\n\nAll occurrences of eta_j cancel in this identity. Thus for any two\nqueried indices of the same parity,\n\n    product_(k=i)^(j-1)t_k<=1.                      (25)\n\nThis is a necessary remote-row restriction, irrespective of how the\ncorrelations and support ratios are selected from the full word.\nIt is NOT asserted sufficient for interpolation.\n\nIf n is odd, its last queried index n-1 is even. Taking (i,j)=(0,n-1)\nin (25), or using the empty product for n=1, gives\n\n    q_(n-1)/q_0<=product_(i=0)^(n-2)b_i\n                        <=product_(i=0)^(n-2)(h_i-1). (26)\n\nThe last inequality follows individually from eta_i>1 and\neta_(i+1)>1. Thus no fixed positive floor eta_i-1 is needed; the\nbound remains uniform when every reserve ratio tends to zero.\n\nFor a final physical step ell=h_(n-1) in (1,2), smoothness gives\n\n    2f_n<=2f_(n-1)-ell*(2-ell)*q_(n-1)\n          =[2(eta_(n-1)-1)+(ell-1)^2]*q_(n-1)\n          <[(ell-1)^2+2(ell-1)]*q_(n-1).           (27)\n\nThis uses the actual terminal value, without specifying its gradient\nor requiring any terminal analogue of (21). Equations (26)--(27)\nare the complete-class obstruction used next.\n\n## 8. Exact comparison on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words\n\nFor m>=4 define\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,\n    c=sigma*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.     (28)\n\nThe word is exactly h=H_m, with the SAME enclosing m in every child.\nIts smallest and last step is ell=1+a, and 0<a<1/2, so ell<2.\nThe offset product is\n\n    D_m:=product_(h_i in H_m)(h_i-1)\n        =a^(N-1)*c^(N-m-1)\n        =beta^(N-1)*c^(-m).                         (29)\n\nOne direct proof is induction from D_0=1 and\nD_(j+1)=D_j^2*a*c^j. The displayed expression at general j is\na^(2^j-1)c^(2^j-j-1). Thus the product through the last queried\ntransition, omitting the final physical step, is D_m/a.\n\nFor EVERY legal instance in Section 7, divide (27) by the true\neta_0^2*q_0 and apply (26) and (29). It follows that\n\n    V <= (a+2)*beta^(N-1)*c^(-m).                   (30)\n\nThe strict version follows when n>=1 and the ratios in (21) are\nstrict, but the weak inequality is the convenient uniform statement.\nIt holds uniformly over arbitrary horizon-dependent correlations,\namplitudes, functions, dimensions and eta_i satisfying the class\nhypotheses. The terminal is entirely free.\n\nLet B_n and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split denominator and\nbenchmark in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`. That fact gives\nB_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<1+4*3^m. Since beta^(N-1)<=exp(-(N-1)/m),\n\n    B_n*V <= (a+2)*(1+4*3^m)*c^(-m)*exp(-(2^m-1)/m)\n             ->0.                                  (31)\n\nFor completeness, c>2 and a+2<5/2 bound the logarithm of all factors\nexcept the final exponential by a constant plus m*log(3/2).\nThe quantity 2^m/m divided by m tends to infinity. This proves the\nlimit without a numerical evaluation or a benchmark replacement.\n\nEquation (31) closes this precise class as a source of a matching\nlower witness on the original diagnostic words. It does NOT show\nsmall unrestricted W_n(H_m), and it does NOT decide the original\nall-schedule exact-profile Goal.\n\n## 9. Exact failure of local-only interpolation checks\n\nTake the physical word h=(3,10) and eta=2, but now choose the legal\nMarkov correlations r_0=-1/sqrt(2), r_1=-1/2. Their corresponding\nparameters in (23) are b=(1/2,4), t=(1/4,1). They have t_0*t_1=1/4,\nso even the same-parity reverse-row condition holds. Use\n\n    g_0=e_0,\n    g_1=(-e_0+e_1)/4,\n    g_2=(e_0-e_1)/4+sqrt(3/8)*e_2,\n    X_0=2e_0, X_1=-e_0, X_2=(3e_0-5e_1)/2,\n    q=(1,1/8,1/2), f=(3/2,3/16,3/4).\n\nThese are literal real coordinates, with every innovation nonzero,\nand every sample has <X_i,g_i>=2q_i and delta_i=q_i>0.\nAll optimum rows pass. The adjacent rows are\n\n    Q_01=-7/8, Q_10=-5/4,\n    Q_12=-11/8, Q_21=-1/4.\n\nThe remote reverse row also passes: Q_20=-3/4. But\n\n    Q_02=3/4>0.                                    (32)\n\nFor a direct check, f_0-f_2=3/4,\n<g_0,X_2-X_0>=-1/2, and ||g_0-g_2||^2=1.\nThus (32) follows from (1). There is no static convex 1-smooth\ninterpolant for these jets. This is a counterexample ONLY to the\nlocal-only sufficiency claim, not a legal GD counterexample to any\ntheorem about actual functions.\n\n## 10. The remaining interface and a priced escape identity\n\nChanging eta_i alone does not escape (30) while the other hypotheses\nof Section 7 hold. The crucial conditions are aligned initial support,\nthe Markov product relation, h_i>eta_i>1, and tight optimum upper rows.\nActual transverse displacement, positive affine values and varying\ndependence have all been included in the excluded class.\n\nOne precise escape is to retain nonzero optimum slack. Define\n\n    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2>=0,\n    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,\n    f_i=(eta_i-1/2)*q_i-z_i.                         (33)\n\nUnder the same aligned-anchor Markov support calculation, the actual\nreverse row becomes, by expanding its value difference,\n\n    Q_(j,i)=(eta_i-1)*q_i\n             [product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k)-1]+z_i-z_j. (34)\n\nPositive reserves require z_i<(eta_i-1)*q_i. Thus it is invalid to\nkeep the product amplification while dropping its cost z_j in the\nterminal value. Another possible escape introduces an initial anchor\nwith correlations to future innovations, in which case the support\nrecursion (23) and (24) acquire additional terms. Allowing support\nratios to cross physical step sizes also changes the sign/parity\nargument. None of these broader interfaces is closed by this draft.\n\nThe next round should preserve the actual law, every old/new row,\nthe true distance and the slack cost in (33)--(34). A generic free\nGram matrix or a finite prescribed-word success would not close the\narbitrary-h quantifier gap. No new computational or verifier allowance\nis inferred from this candidate.\n\n## 11. Literature and proof-dependency audit\n\nThe final memory audit also recovered the older UNVERIFIED public\nfindings `c035d2edbaffb2c0` and `fd7036b719ef9645`. Their full domains\nand coordinate definitions were read. The former treats first\noccurrences of consecutive ruler levels, with constant gradients in\nthe intervening ordinary blocks and a common queried terminal cell;\nit already includes positive heights and free affine intercepts in\nthat domain. The latter prices a negative-then-positive fresh relay.\nThose are relevant prior boundaries, not premises of this manuscript.\n\nC97 instead changes gradients at EVERY actual physical sample and\nleaves the entire final completion free in its class bound. Its\nrestriction 1<eta_i<h_i and tight optimum upper rows is stronger in\na different direction. We do not claim that positive affine Markov\nrelays themselves are new, or that C97 subsumes either older family.\nFuture slack or mixed-sign work must compare its complete domain with\nthose older interfaces before claiming an escape.\n\nMatlas searches for nonstationary binary Markov product correlations\nreturned Gaussian graph, noncommutative exchangeability and resistance\ndistance results, none applicable to the interpolation problem. The\nfirst built-in web attempt returned: \"Fatal error: connection failed:\nerror sending request\". A later search located Tuncer Kaya, *An exact\nalternative solution method of 1D Ising model with Block-spin\ntransformation at H=0*, arXiv:1801.04452 (2018). The exact PDF was\ndownloaded to downloads/ising_binary_bonds_1801.04452.pdf; the browser\nextraction is saved alongside it and Sections I--II and the displayed\nderivation through the correlation recursion were read.\n\nThat paper concerns spin correlations for an Ising chain. Those\nstatements do not establish any GD interpolation inequality, positive\nvalue reserve, minimizer distance, or prescribed-word payoff. We use\nno external theorem or proof assertion from it. The finite independent-\nbond law (4), its normalization and its product moments are proved\ndirectly above. Sections 3--10 likewise use only the two accepted\npredecessors named at the start and their fully displayed new algebra.\nThis is one joint unverified manuscript, not a chain of unverified\nintermediate facts.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "015ee2bf5743d52b", "branch_id": "C97_positive_height_markov", "status": "complete_joint_unverified_not_fact", "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c97_positive_height_markov.md", "accepted_fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "related_unverified_boundaries": ["250d197b0a861a30", "db5c6ee920bcc660", "b4daf40dbd6a3105", "dce7894e98c813d9", "c035d2edbaffb2c0", "fd7036b719ef9645"], "runtime_round": 1, "completed_after_return": 28, "completed_ceiling": 29, "new_fact_submit": 0, "new_numerical_evaluations": 0}, "glossary": {"eta_M": "Constant support ratio eta in Sections2-6 of C97; 1<eta<min h.", "eta_M_i": "Varying support ratio <X_i,g_i>/||g_i||^2 in C97 Sections7-10.", "theta_M": "Common strict innovation parameter theta in (0,1).", "b_M_i": "(h_i-eta)/eta in the construction; (h_i-eta_i)/eta_(i+1) in the larger class.", "t_M_i": "Positive chronological gradient coefficient magnitude t_i; construction theta*min{1,(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1)}.", "r_M_i": "Binary transition correlation r_i; construction -sqrt(t_i/b_i).", "P_M_ij": "Signed product of -t_k over i<=k<j.", "E_M_ij": "Squared-amplitude ratio q_j/q_i, the product t_k*b_k.", "z_M_i": "Optimum upper-row slack <X_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.", "delta_M_i": "Positive affine reserve f_i-||g_i||^2/2.", "D_M": "Exact offset product over original H_m, beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m).", "All remaining C97 symbols": "Defined in order in the complete evidence; no unverified predecessor is used."}}
{"id": "08a8af0b09a1b7f9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T13:29:58.646975+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max34 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED retained-history result: the legal positive-height prefix(1,6), followed by bridge13/2, forces the full first gradient to e. No all-word or minimax conclusion.", "evidence": "# Retained affine returns: an empty joint transition and its paid boundary repair\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, analytical round34.\nNo numerical, solver, replay, machine-algebra, or formal-verifier call was\nmade. No unverified finding is used as a premise. The target remains the\noriginal arbitrary-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon exact-profile\nquestion, with a threshold independent of the word. This document does\nnot settle that question.\n\nThe result is a concrete legal-history obstruction to universally using\ntwo consecutive constant-gradient cells with a positive reversal scale.\nIt allows the entire old parallel component, both affine offsets, every\nfresh dimension, and even a changed common minimum. On a neighboring\nrange it solves the joint choices exactly within the retained two-plane\nenvelope family and prices the changed minimum and closest distance.\nNeither result is a claim of an all-depth improving policy.\n\nAccepted inputs: the smooth-convex interpolation inequality and its\norientation from fact1252b8823bb5a290; the same-word Huber/single-cut\nbaseline from factb98bb807d514e80d; and the exact B_n recursion from\nfact0be8b54e0a33381c. All other arguments appear below.\n\n## 1. Notation and the static interpolation construction\n\nWork with smoothness1 in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, with unit\nvector e. Scalar coordinates mean multiples of e; extra coordinates may\nbe present. Write t=<e,x>. For a finite convex maximum J of affine planes,\nits envelope is\n\n    f(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                         (1.1)\n\nIf a plane of normal g is active at z and x=z+g, then its supporting\ninequality, with the square expanded, shows z uniquely minimizes (1.1).\nThe resulting value is J(z)+||g||^2/2 and the gradient is g. To justify\nthe last assertion directly, let z(x) be the unique proximal minimizer\nand g(x)=x-z(x). Existence follows from coercivity, and uniqueness from\nthe strictly convex quadratic. Adding the two subgradient inequalities\nfor J at z(x),z(y) gives\n\n    ||g(x)-g(y)||^2 <= <g(x)-g(y),x-y>.\n\nThe supporting inequality at z(x) gives\nf(y)>=f(x)+<g(x),y-x>, while evaluating the proximal objective for y\nat z(x) gives the upper remainder bound||y-x||^2/2. Thus f is\ndifferentiable with derivative g; the displayed inequality proves its Lipschitz\nconstant is at most1. The envelope is convex because its defining\nobjective is jointly convex. Its minimum and minimizer set coincide\nwith those of J: equality in J(z)+||x-z||^2/2>=min J requires x=z and\nJ(z)=min J. These arguments also cover affine ties and flat minimizer\nsets. Every construction below specifies one J before the whole run.\n\nFor actual smooth jets use the accepted signed residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.      (1.2)\n\nIn proximal coordinates z_i=x_i-g_i and H_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n(1.2) is precisely the supporting-plane inequality\n\n    H_j >= H_i+<g_i,z_j-z_i>.\n\nConsequently checking every active affine plane at every proposed\nproximal sample checks both directions of every original interpolation\npair, not just adjacent gradient inequalities.\n\n## 2. A legal positive-height history and its exact retained state\n\nSet\n\n    J_old(z)=|<e,z>|=max(0,<e,z>,-<e,z>),\n    f_old=env J_old,       x_init=3e.\n\nThe scalar formula is f_old(te)=t^2/2 for |t|<=1 and |t|-1/2\nfor |t|>=1. Its gradient is clip(t,-1,1)e. The minimizer set is\nthe hyperplane {<e,z>=0}; its true distance from x_init is3.\n\nApply the prefix steps(1,6). Its queried data, including the sample\nused for the next bridge, are\n\n    x_A=3e,   g_A=e,    f_A=5/2,   z_A=2e,    H_A=2;\n    x_B=2e,   g_B=e,    f_B=3/2,   z_B=e,     H_B=1;\n    x_C=-4e,  g_C=-e,   f_C=7/2,   z_C=-3e,  H_C=3.     (2.1)\n\nEach selected plane is active at its displayed proximal point; the\nother signed plane is nonpositive there. This checks all old pairs.\nThe two updates are 3e-e=2e and 2e-6e=-4e. In particular every old\nqueried proximal height is strictly positive, all positions are\ndistinct, and the prefix is a real run of one static function.\n\nBefore the next bridge the outgoing state is p=-3e, u=e, G=1, with\ncurrent query p-G u=-4e. The complete profiles, in the notation of\nthe assigned retained-state interface, are\n\n    Phi(t)=J_old(p+t u)=|t-3|,\n    Psi(s)=min(2-5s,1-4s,3),\n    Xi(t,r)=(t-6)^2    if r>=0 and |t-3|<=r,\n             +infinity otherwise.                       (2.2)\n\nHere Xi is the squared distance from x_init to the slice of the\nsublevel {J_old<=r} with <u,z-p>=t. Indeed that coordinate fixes\n<e,z>=t-3, and setting every perpendicular coordinate to zero\nminimizes the distance. At r=0 the unique permitted t is3, and the\nsquared distance is9. Thus neither a parallel component nor the\nsublevel distance has been reset.\n\nApply the prescribed bridge13/2 at x_C. The next start is\n\n    X=-4e+(13/2)e=(5/2)e.                                (2.3)\n\nIt lies strictly inside the previously sampled equal-gradient\nsegment[2e,3e], rather than repeating an old point.\n\n## 3. Full-vector rigidity at the first new cell\n\nLet F be ANY convex differentiable1-smooth function in ANY enlarged\nfinite dimension preserving the three jets(2.1), up to a common\nadditive constant. It need not have the old minimum or the old\nfunction away from the queries.\n\nFor 2<=t<=3, convexity between x_B and x_A gives\nF(te)<=t-1/2, after subtracting that common constant. The supporting\ninequality at x_B gives the reverse inequality. Hence\n\n    F(te)=t-1/2.\n\nUsing the old-to-new instance of(1.2), with v=grad F(te), gives\n\n    Q_(B,t)=||v-e||^2/2 <=0.\n\nTherefore v=e as a FULL vector. Every perpendicular component vanishes.\n\nNow prescribe any nonnegative first ordinary list with total\n\n    0<=S<=1/2.\n\nAll its partial sums are in[0,S]. By induction every position is\n(5/2-u)e in[2e,5e/2], so every gradient in this actual block, including\nits endpoint query, is forced to be e. This conclusion does not even\nassume beforehand that the first cell is constant. In the proposed\ntwo-plane parameterization\n\n    ell_1(z)=a_1+<q,z>,    ell_2(z)=a_2-beta<q,z>,\n\nit forces q=q_0=e, zero fresh energy, and a_1=0 in the original\nvalue normalization when ell_1 is the designated active plane. A\nredundant first plane could instead have a_1<0, but then it lies below\nthe already retained plane z and cannot affect any query or minimum.\nThe active-plane assertion follows from\nf(te)=t-1/2, z=(t-1)e and J(z)=t-1. If all values are recentered by\na common minimum mu, the first intercept becomes-mu; it is the same\nphysical plane and not an additional degree of freedom.\n\n## 4. A quantitative obstruction to the remaining reversal\n\nAfter that block prescribe a checkpoint b>1, and let\n\n    Y=5/2-S-b.                                           (4.1)\n\nThe next position is exactly Ye. Suppose the proposed second cell\nstarts with gradient-beta e, beta>=0. Apply(1.2) in BOTH directions\nbetween this actual next sample and the retained query B. Function\nvalues and all common additive constants cancel, leaving\n\n    Q_(B,Y)+Q_(Y,B)=(1+beta)(Y-1+beta).                  (4.2)\n\nThus if Y>1 there is NO beta>=0, no choice of either affine offset,\nand no transverse innovation or change of minimum that makes the\ntwo-cell rule legal. If Y=1 there is NO beta>0. This is an obstruction\nfor arbitrary smooth-convex extensions, stronger than just an empty\ninterval for a selected max-affine construction.\n\nWhen Y>1, at least one original ordered row has residual at least\n(Y-1)/2. This is a quantitative gap in the actual original rows.\nIt is not a formal-vector or numerically estimated separator.\nFor an unrestricted next gradient v, the same pair sum gives\n\n    ||v-(Y/2)e||^2 <= (2-Y)^2/4.                         (4.3)\n\nIn the relevant range1<Y<3/2 this implies <v,e>>=Y-1>0.\nAllowing the first next gradient to change direction cannot permit a\nnonpositive e component without changing the retained history.\n\nThe explicit obstruction parameters are\n\n    S=1/5, b=6/5, Y=11/10, T=1/20,                    (4.4)\n\nwhere T is the second ordinary-block mass. The pair gap is\n(1+beta)(1/10+beta), and some original row is at least1/20.\nThe checkpoint is strictly larger than1, yet violates the former\ncondition b>=T+2. Even the purely local necessary reversal constraint\nb-1>=(T+1)beta allows beta=1/20 here. The failure is therefore an\nactual retained-history constraint, not merely b<=1 or a missing\nlocal norm condition. Any second-block mass would give the same\nobstruction at its first sample.\n\n## 5. The omitted datum is an old query, not a missing plane\n\nIt is useful to see a fully explicit false local completion. Delete\nonly the requirement to interpolate the old jet B, retaining A and C\nand their planes, and define in the same scalar space\n\n    J_drop(z)=max(|z|,5/4-z/20).                         (5.1)\n\nThis is an actual static polyhedral function. It retains the complete\nold envelope |z|. At z_A=2 and z_C=-3, the added plane has values\n23/20<=2 and7/5<=3. Thus both remaining old jets are unchanged.\n\nOn the first new block of(4.4), proximal coordinates lie in\n[13/10,3/2]. The added plane is at most the positive plane throughout,\nbecause 5/4<=(21/20)(13/10)=273/200. Its gradient is therefore1.\nAt the second block, with proposed gradient-1/20, proximal coordinates\nlie in[23/20,461/400]. There the new plane dominates |z| because\n\n    5/4>(21/20)(461/400)=9681/8000,\n\nand these coordinates are positive. Endpoints suffice because each\nplane comparison is affine in the partial sum. This checks every\nremaining old/new and new/new comparison, including both directions.\nThe new queried heights above the actual minimum are positive: the\nminimum is mu_drop=25/21, while the largest second proximal coordinate\n461/400 is strictly below25/21. The new plane decreases to its\nminimum at25/21.\n\nThe omitted old query fails explicitly:\n\n    J_drop(z_B)>=5/4-1/20=6/5>H_B=1.                    (5.2)\n\nIn fact env J_drop has gradient17/21 at x_B=2 and value1339/882,\nwhereas the required gradient and value are1 and3/2. Its minimum is\n25/21 and its true distance from x_init=3 is38/21.\n\nThis does NOT produce a legal run of the original complete word,\nbecause B is queried by that word and its altered gradient changes\nthe subsequent update. It proves exactly why deleting the query is\ninvalid. The geometric profile Phi, the old planes, the boundary G,\nand Xi of the input remain the same if B is omitted from the query\nlist; Psi loses its term1-4s. That term records the decisive offset\ncap. No insufficiency of the FULL retained state is asserted.\n\n## 6. Exact joint transition on the neighboring feasible range\n\nKeep the same old history, first bridge13/2, and first ordinary mass\nS in[0,1/2]. Now take b=5/2-S-Y with\n\n    0<=Y<1,\n\nand any second ordinary mass T>=0. Such b is greater than1. Retain\nJ_old and allow exactly the two new planes of Section3, with arbitrary\noffsets and the prescribed opposite gradients. The rigidity proof\nforces the first plane to be z. Hence the entire family reduces,\nwithout dropping any parameter that could help, to\n\n    J(z)=max(|z|,a-beta z),    beta>0.                   (6.1)\n\nThis section optimizes the stated two-plane ENVELOPE family. It is\nnot an upper bound for all other smooth functions preserving the jets.\nWithin this family the previous mass condition fails precisely when\nb<T+2, equivalently T>1/2-S-Y. The formulas here still apply on that\nwhole intersection with the displayed S,Y,T domain. Other legal\nretained histories and Y<0 are not being characterized by this rule.\n\nAll positive old proximal coordinates are at least1 and include1;\nthe newly queried first-block coordinates are at least1 too. The\nnegative old proximal coordinate is-3. Thus all future-plane tests\nat these queries are EXACTLY\n\n    a<=1+beta,       a<=3(1-beta).                       (6.2)\n\nThe second proximal coordinates are Y+t beta,1<=t<=T+1, all positive.\nThere the positive old plane dominates the other old planes. The\nnew-plane comparisons are therefore EXACTLY\n\n    a>=(1+beta)[Y+(T+1)beta].                           (6.3)\n\nIntermediate points add no constraint because the comparisons are\naffine in the partial sum. Equations(6.2)-(6.3) include the first\nnew plane at every second query, the second new plane at EVERY old\nand first query, and all old planes at the new queries. All old/old\nand first/first comparisons are already witnessed by their active\npositive or negative planes. This proves joint all-pair sufficiency\nas well as necessity within(6.1).\n\nEquation(6.3) implies a>0. Equation(6.2) then implies beta<1. Write\n\n    C=T+1, d=2T+1, delta=1-Y, mu=a/(1+beta).\n\nThe exact nonempty parameter region is\n\n    0<beta<1,\n    Y+C beta <= mu <= min(1,3(1-beta)/(1+beta)).          (6.4)\n\nFor any such pair, the actual minimum is mu and the minimizer is\nz=mu in the scalar space. Indeed the positive and new decreasing\nplanes intersect atmu>0, where their common value ismu; both other\nplanes are smaller. The maximum increases on either side of this\ncrossing. In an enlarged space the minimizer set is the hyperplane\n<e,z>=mu, and the true distance from3e is\n\n    R_new=3-mu.                                        (6.5)\n\nThe final position is Y+T beta. Its proximal position is Y+C beta,\nand its value above the ACTUAL common minimum is\n\n    f_end-mu=beta(mu-Y)-(T+1/2)beta^2.\n\nThus the exact normalized payoff is\n\n    V(mu,beta)=[2beta(mu-Y)-d beta^2]/(3-mu)^2.           (6.6)\n\nRaising the minimum must recenter EVERY old queried value and height\nby this same mu. It does not change any gradient or prescribed step.\nIf the old minimum0 is instead required to remain a minimizer, the\nintercept must satisfy a<=0, which contradicts(6.3). Thus the change\nof minimum is necessary on this Y>=0 branch, not an optional gain\ninserted into its normalization.\n\n## 7. Solve the offset and reversal scale together\n\nAt a feasible beta the numerator of(6.6) is at least beta^2, by the\nleft inequality in(6.4). Differentiating in mu gives\n\n    partial_mu V=2beta/(3-mu)^2\n                   +2[2beta(mu-Y)-d beta^2]/(3-mu)^3>0.\n\nHence choose the largest permitted mu, not an independent first-cell\noptimum. For 0<beta<=1/2 it equals1. For beta>1/2 it equals\n3(1-beta)/(1+beta), and(6.6) becomes\n\n    V=[(6-2Y)/beta-(6+2Y)beta\n                        -d(1+2beta+beta^2)-4Y]/36.     (7.1)\n\nIts derivative is\n\n    [-(6-2Y)/beta^2-(6+2Y)-2d(1+beta)]/36<0.\n\nIf any beta>1/2 is feasible, beta=1/2 is feasible as well: then\nY+C/2<Y+C beta<=mu<1. Thus no omitted larger-beta component can\nimprove on its boundary.\n\nOn(0,1/2], the payoff is[2delta beta-d beta^2]/4. The exact global\njoint optimum over(6.4) is\n\n    mu_opt=1,\n    beta_opt=min(1/2,delta/d),\n    V_opt=[2delta beta_opt-d beta_opt^2]/4.              (7.2)\n\nThis beta is feasible. If beta_opt=delta/d, then C<=d gives\nC beta_opt<=delta. If beta_opt=1/2, then delta>=d/2=C-1/2>=C/2.\nThese cover T=0 too. An attained optimum is being given for the\nclosed nonnegative-height family, not merely a stationarity formula.\n\nFor strictly positive retained heights, mu=1 would turn H_B into0.\nIn the branch delta/d<=1/2, a concrete strictly positive choice is\n\n    0<epsilon<delta, mu=1-epsilon,\n    beta=(delta-epsilon)/d,\n    a=(1+beta)mu. This also excludes\nany alternative path through0 without an enumeration or an assumption.\n\nFor completeness, the excesses over S_0 in the order via2, via1,\nand direct are\n\n    2U[q+q^2+e-e^2],\n    2B[w^2-mw+m^2+m+t-t^2],\n    2Ue(1-e)+2Bt(1-t).                            (16)\n\nThe first and third are nonnegative by0<=e,t<=1 and q>=0.\nFor the second use w^2-mw+m^2=(w-m/2)^2+3m^2/4>=0.\nThis proves the nonnegative-distance claim used above without\nassuming the candidate paths are shortest in advance.\n\n## 5. Exact incompatible-order value minimum\n\nRetain J_s from(11), and define\n\n    J_f^-=a(2-a)+r(2-r)m^2+b(2-b)t^2+d(2-d)w^2\n                         +2(d-1)[a*t*(1-t)+r*w*(m-w)],\n\n    P=2a(b-1)[q+q^2+e-e^2],\n    Q=2a(d-1)[w^2-mw+m^2+m+t-t^2],\n    R=2a(b-1)e(1-e), S=2a(d-1)t(1-t).             (17)\n\nThe entire all-value minimum is\n\n    V_min=(u^2/b^2)J_s+(v^2/d^2)J_f^-\n          -min{(u^2/b^2)P,(v^2/d^2)Q,\n                                (u^2/b^2)R+(v^2/d^2)S}. (18)\n\nIndeed the only joint distance that can exceed its separate-mode sum\nis D_0, whose sink weight is2a. Multiplying(16) by2a and using(4)\ngives exactly the three charges in(18). The separately optimized fast\nvalues exceed ||g_i^f||^2/2 by2Bt(1-t) at0 and2Bw(m-w) at1.\nSubstitution in(3) gives J_f^- and proves the full formula.\n\nFor1<d<a<b<r, with strict inequalities in(1) and nonzero u,v, all\nthree charges are strictly positive. In particular e lies in(0,1),\nm>0, and t lies in(0,1). Thus the joint optimized energy really is\nSTRICTLY less than the sum of the two complete scalar minima. This\npositive price alone is not evidence of new full-cone information.\nSection7 proves that even its FULL optimized effect cannot be negative.\n\nAt a=d, m=t=w=0, Q=0 and(18) equals(12); thus the two formula branches\nagree. If d=1, B=Q=S=0 and the fast mode contributes no slack cost.\nIf either amplitude vanishes, at least one of the first two charges\nvanishes and the joint price increment is zero, as required.\n\n## 6. Full amplitude elimination and the parameter minimum\n\nNo normalization is needed to decide the energy sign. To make the\namplitude minimum a finite number, temporarily set u^2+v^2=1 and\ntau=u^2 in[0,1]. This is merely a scale convention for the primitive\nenergy, NOT a replacement for the problem's minimizer-distance\nnormalization. The actual closest-minimizer distance is supplied below.\n\nWhen a<=d, the exact amplitude-and-value minimum is\n\n    min{J_s/b^2,J_f^+/d^2},                        (19)\n\nattained by the corresponding pure amplitude, or every mixture when\nthe two coefficients agree.\n\nWhen a>=d, put A=J_s/b^2, E=J_f^-/d^2 and define three affine functions\n\n    L1(tau)=E+tau*(A-E-P/b^2),\n    L2(tau)=E-Q/d^2+tau*(A-E+Q/d^2),\n    L3(tau)=E-S/d^2+tau*(A-R/b^2-E+S/d^2).         (20)\n\nThen(18) is max{L1(tau),L2(tau),L3(tau)}. Write\nLj(tau)=c_j+m_j*tau using exactly the coefficients in(20), and form\n\n    K={0,1} union\n      {(c_k-c_j)/(m_j-m_k): j<k, m_j!=m_k,\n                                  0<=(c_k-c_j)/(m_j-m_k)<=1}. (21)\n\nThe exact amplitude-and-value minimum is the explicit finite expression\n\n    min_(tau in K) max{L1(tau),L2(tau),L3(tau)}.    (22)\n\nThere are at most five candidates. This formula requires no search,\nroot selection, or positivity presumption about a denominator. Parallel\nlines contribute no intersection; coincident lines need no separate\ncandidate. To prove it, their intersection points divide[0,1] into\nintervals on each of which the maximum is affine. An affine function\nhas a minimum at an endpoint unless constant, in which case an endpoint\nstill attains it. The partition endpoints are exactly a subset of K.\n\nEquations(19)--(22) decide every amplitude and every value parameter\nfor every word in(1). Varying b,d throughout(1) already varies every\ncurvature in the specified family; no additional spectral optimization\nis silently fixed. Section7 proves the resulting expression nonnegative\nuniformly on the whole word domain.\n\nIf one also minimizes over ALL words in(1), the normalized minimum\nis exactly zero. Nonnegativity is Section7. Attainment occurs at\na=r=b=d=2: both modes have curvature1/2, x_i=g_i=0 for i>=1,\nand the optimized f_0=||g_0||^2/2 gives V=0 for any u^2+v^2=1.\nThis boundary word need not pass the older full-cone tests; this\nstatement is only the complete parameter minimum of the stated family.\n\n## 7. Complete comparison: the local energy already pays every price\n\nThe terminal sample is(0,0,0), so EVERY legal value assignment obeys\n\n    f_i-f_4>=||g_i||^2/2                           (23)\n\nby Q_(4,i)<=0. Substituting into the full energy gives\n\n    V>=sum_(i<4)h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2.              (24)\n\nThe b and d terms are nonnegative because b,d<=2. The first two\nterms are nonnegative separately for EACH curvature theta in[1/b,1],\na slightly larger interval than the two actual curvatures. For such\na mode, put q_theta=1-a*theta. Its first two contributions, divided\nby its squared initial gradient when nonzero, are\n\n    a(2-a)+r(2-r)q_theta^2.                        (25)\n\nA vanished initial gradient contributes zero and needs no division.\nIf r<=2, every coefficient in(24) is nonnegative. Suppose r>2 and\nwrite N=r(r-2)>0. If q_theta>=0, then\n\n    0<=q_theta<=1-a/b<=1-a/2,\n    N<=a(a+2),\n    a(2-a)-N*q_theta^2\n       >=a(2-a)-a(a+2)(1-a/2)^2\n        =a^3(2-a)/4>=0.                            (26)\n\nThe middle inequality follows from r<=a+2 and the monotonicity of\nr(r-2) for r>=2. This argument includes a=0 and a=2.\n\nIf q_theta<0, then a>1 and\n\n    |q_theta|<=a-1<=b-1,\n    N(b-1)^2=[(r-1)^2-1](b-1)^2\n                      <=1-(b-1)^2=b(2-b),\n    a(2-a)>=b(2-b).                                (27)\n\nThe last line uses1<a<=b<=2, where x(2-x) decreases. Consequently\na(2-a)-N*q_theta^2>=0 here too. No continuity argument that discards\na sign or a zero product is used. Equations(24)--(27) prove\n\n                         V_min>=0                 (28)\n\nfor the ENTIRE family and ALL legal nonseparable values. More explicitly,\nthe proof gives the lower bound\n\n V_min >= (u^2/b^2)[a(2-a)+r(2-r)e^2+b(2-b)q^2]\n          +(v^2/d^2)[a(2-a)+r(2-r)z_1^2\n                                    +b(2-b)z_2^2+d(2-d)z_3^2], (29)\n\nwhose two first-pair brackets are nonnegative by(26)--(27).\n\nThis settles the comparison with the COMPLETE separate scalar family,\nnot only the two selected curvatures. For any fixed h, let the full\nseparate scalar tests range over every curvature in[0,1], every initial\namplitude and every admissible scalar value assignment, with every\ndirected pair retained. Regardless of the signs of any of those tests,\n(28) holds for every joint trajectory in our family. Therefore their\nentire conjunction implies(28); so does its conjunction with every old\ncut, clipping, triangle, coupled-energy or other full-cone necessary\ncondition. No parameter region surviving those older conditions can\nbe newly excluded by THIS family on(1).\n\nOne domain condition has a short independent connection to the older\ntests. The ordinary second-cut scalar test implies r<a+2 for any full\ncertificate. For r>1 let eta=(r-1)/(1+b+d), choose the static scalar\nderivative clip(x,[-eta,1]), and start at x_0=1+a. Its points are\n\n    x=(1+a,1,-eta*(1+b+d),-eta*(1+d),-eta),\n    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).\n\nEvery value is f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2, with f(0)=0, and x_4=g_4.\nThe derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, so this\nis a legal single static function. Telescoping\nx_(i+1)^2=x_i^2-2h_i*x_i*g_i+h_i^2*g_i^2 in(3) gives\nV=(1+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. Thus a full certificate requires\nC(r-1)<=(1+a)(1+b+d). Using C=1+a+r+b+d, this rearranges to\n\n    r(C+a+1)<=(a+2)C-a(a+1)<(a+2)(C+a+1).\n\nIt follows that r<a+2. The r<=1 case already satisfies that bound.\nThis derivation is included so no old unverified prefix lemma is used.\nThe central-product condition remains explicitly part of our family;\nwe make no additional global-necessity claim for it here.\n\nThere is no claim that two separately nonnegative scalar energies\ngenerally imply nonnegative joint energy. Formula(18) explicitly\nsubtracts a sometimes strictly positive joint price. The structural\nreason for redundancy is instead(23)--(27): the terminal gradient is\nzero, the last two local step coefficients are nonnegative, and the\nfirst two local coefficients are already compensated for the entire\navailable spectral interval. The common-path case in Section3 is an\nadditional exact explanation, not a missing general coupling argument.\n\n## 8. One attaining static function and its true distance\n\nUse the exact D_i above and values\n\n    f_i=F_base(x_i)-D_i, f_4=0.\n\nEvery ordered Q inequality was checked in Section2, and x_4=g_4=0.\nDefine on the same physical Euclidean plane\n\n    c_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i,\n    H(y)=max_(0<=i<=4){c_i+<x_i,y-g_i>+||y-g_i||^2/2},\n    F(x)=sup_y{<x,y>-H(y)}.                        (30)\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2, establishes that this is\nan everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth function, with\nF(x_i)=f_i and grad F(x_i)=g_i. Its necessity convention is exactly\nour Q<=0, as checked above. The zero terminal is an actual minimizer.\nConsequently the SAME one static function realizes all four updates\nand all five values, including all nonseparable optimal values in(18).\n\nThe full minimizer set can be stated without assuming uniqueness.\nWrite y_i=x_i-g_i, and let\n\n    A_active={i: D_i=w_4i}.\n\nSince a direct path is available, D_i<=w_4i. Expanding(30) gives\n\n    H(y)=||y||^2/2+\n                 max_i{<y_i,y>+D_i-w_4i}.\n\nThus H(0)=0 and\n\n    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A_active}.              (31)\n\nTo justify the last identity,0 maximizes<x,y>-H(y) precisely when\nx is a subgradient of H at0. The active affine pieces there are\nexactly A_active. Their convex hull is the subdifferential: hull\nmembers satisfy the subgradient inequality by convex averaging; a\npoint outside the hull is strictly separated from the finite compact\nhull, and a sufficiently small displacement in that separating\ndirection violates the inequality. Inactive pieces remain below\nzero for sufficiently small displacements. This proves both inclusions.\n\nIn particular the true squared initial distance is\n\n    R_true^2=dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A_active})^2.    (32)\n\nThis is also an explicit finite formula if desired. When x_0!=0 it\nis outside the hull because g_0!=0, while every hull point minimizes\nF. In the plane a closest hull point lies on its boundary, hence on\na segment between two of its vertices; a point or segment hull is\nincluded. Therefore take the minimum over all active pairs i,j,\nincluding i=j, of the following segment distances. For y_i!=y_j put\n\n    theta_ij=clip(<x_0-y_i,y_j-y_i>/||y_j-y_i||^2,[0,1]),\n    R_ij^2=||x_0-y_i-theta_ij*(y_j-y_i)||^2;\n\nfor y_i=y_j put R_ij^2=||x_0-y_i||^2. Then R_true^2=min R_ij^2.\nEvery tested segment lies inside the hull and the boundary edges are\namong them, proving equality. This handles coincident points without\na zero denominator. If x_0=0 the complete trajectory is zero and\nR_true=0; it is not a nontrivial normalized adversary.\n\nThe closest minimizer can therefore be used to normalize an actual\nnonzero transcript if needed. This does not convert(28), a primitive\nenergy comparison, into a statement about the original minimax W.\n\n## 9. Boundaries, structural conclusion and the next interface\n\nThe formulas retain the original indices0..4, including the duplicated\nsamples at a=0. All twenty directed finite inequalities remain in the\nvalue problem. The flow keeps the original terminal source. Products\nthat vanish at a=b, a=d, r=b, r=d, or b=d are allowed; vanished\namplitudes, unit curvature, zero slack, tied shortest paths, coincident\nsamples and equality in either condition of(1) are explicitly covered.\nThe two-dimensional physical family is an adversary class, not a\nrank restriction imposed on the full formal R^5 PSD seed problem.\n\nThe exact three-path price can be positive, yet the COMPLETE family\nminimum is nonnegative. Changing its amplitudes, taking a different\ncase of the same min, or perturbing its two annihilation steps cannot\ncreate new information within(1). This closure is genuinely beyond\nthe single-reversal family: its interior has two or three fast sign\nreversals and a separately changing slow mode.\n\nWhat remains outside this result is precise:\n\n* a terminal mode with g_4!=0, where the term-sC||g_4||^2 survives and\n  node4 can no longer be used as an optimum in(23);\n* differently ordered killing/reversal schedules outside(1), including\n  (r-1)(b-1)>1 or r>a+2, unless an independently proved older condition\n  excludes that word;\n* additional modes not killed by either b or d, nonlinear repaired\n  geometry, and the complete unrestricted h/Lambda/full-PSD problem.\n\nThe next authorized analytical round should address retained nonzero\nterminal history with multiple reversals, not another choice of roots,\namplitudes or paths inside this closed terminal-zero theorem. A proposed quantitative eps escape is proved EXACTLY equivalent to the old unit-quadratic test on its precise first-step slice, so it supplies no independent exclusion.", "evidence": "# The matched equality locus: exact branches, boundary exclusions, and an old-test identity\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED.
